# SECCIÓN 1.3 — Siempre persigues metas: asignadas o autogeneradas


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-10: Assignments (assigned goals, assigned identity)
- idea: "Assignment" es todo lo que te dan y no cuestionas (escuela, empleo, cualquier camino prefijado). Las assignments se acumulan en responsabilidades que se comen tu tiempo, tu dinero y tus recursos, hasta que cambiar se vuelve muy difícil. "There is absolutely no set path that someone can give you" para forjar tu propio camino: si te lo dan, es una assignment. Como "we are goal-oriented creatures", si no creas tu meta inviertes energía en la identidad asignada (estudiante universitario, título corporativo, "retired at 65 dude"), y la información que te programa construye "an identity that you didn't create". Bajo estrés, que es el estado del 99%, la gente adopta metas asignadas y entra en una "hamster wheel" que la hunde más. No sabes lo que quieres porque nunca probaste nada fuera de lo asignado. Matiz clave: las assignments no son malas en sí; son "stepping stones" y "training wheels" que en algún momento hay que quitarse.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: dentro del mismo video de 2024 el autor matiza sus propias frases ("the life of the masses" vs "stepping stones, not evil"). Ver E-02.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-003-165 <= U-003-104 | mismo pasaje casi literal ("if you aren't working towards your ideal future you're working towards someone else's..."); U-003-104 es su reaparición en la compilación "Get Ahead Of 99%..." (2024-03-21) del video de 2023-03-26 ; FUSION: U-026-123 <= U-026-130 | mismo pasaje del mismo video (3:11); U-026-123 ya contiene la frase "any set path someone gives you is by definition an assignment"; la tensión anotada en U-026-130 queda en Evolución (E-02, E-09)

### U-026-123
- **tipo:** término-acuñado
- **titulo:** Assignments: things given to you that you don't question, which pile up into responsibilities that eat your time, money and resources
- **desarrollo:** "As we are growing up as we are going to school as we are getting a job we are given assignments this is what I call something that is assigned to you you don't question and you continue doing it." Consequence: "before you know it you have so many responsibilities that you can't really do anything with your life all of your time is taken up to the point where you don't have the money time or resources to create a better life for yourself", and it is "so much more difficult to actually change". Any set path someone gives you is by definition an assignment: "it was assigned to you it wasn't created by you".
- **ejemplos:** School, jobs.
- **cita:** "this is what I call something that is assigned to you"
- **terminos:** assignment; assigned
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-026-122
- **fuente:** If Your Life Is Spiraling Out Of Control, Here's The Solution To Feeling Lost.md, 0:48-1:33, 3:11, 2024-03-10
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-026-130
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** There is no set path anyone can give you to forge your own path; a given path is an assignment
- **desarrollo:** "There is absolutely no set path that someone can give you to achieve this to get what you want to forge your own path or else that's an assignment it was assigned to you it wasn't created by you."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "there is absolutely no set path that someone can give you"
- **terminos:** assignment; forge your own path
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-026-123
- **fuente:** If Your Life Is Spiraling Out Of Control, Here's The Solution To Feeling Lost.md, 3:11, 2024-03-10
- **tension:** It sits beside his own step-by-step methods and courses; he resolves it in U-026-131 by framing given advice and tools as direction/stepping stones, not destinations.


### U-026-131
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Assignments are stepping stones, not evil: use schools, jobs and advice for direction, then take the training wheels off
- **desarrollo:** "I see assignments as a stepping stone so meaning I see schools and jobs as a stepping stone I don't see them as something to avoid or something that's absolutely evil." People often think he holds the opposite. What he promotes is entrepreneurship, forging your own path, diving into the unknown, solving problems, new experiences, and "not getting locked in a repetitive day-to-day mindless routine that comes from schools and jobs". Those things are helpful: people can give advice and tools for direction, "but inevitably you're going to reach whatever that destination was and it's not going to be as nice as you wanted it to so you have to course correct learn from your mistakes and start to pursue your own path you have to eventually take the training wheels off".
- **ejemplos:** Schools, jobs, advice as training wheels.
- **cita:** "you have to eventually take the training wheels off"
- **terminos:** stepping stone; training wheels; course correct
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-026-123
- **fuente:** If Your Life Is Spiraling Out Of Control, Here's The Solution To Feeling Lost.md, 3:11-4:44, 2024-03-10
- **tension:** Nuances U-026-120 and U-026-123, which describe school and jobs as the "life of the masses"; here they are explicitly not to be avoided.


### U-026-132
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** You don't know what you want because you haven't tried anything outside what's been assigned
- **desarrollo:** "You don't know what you want out of life because you haven't tried anything outside of what's been assigned to you you haven't experimented with new domains you haven't learned outside of high school or college you haven't dug into a lesser explored crevice of reality that isn't taught in schools." So "it's no wonder you don't know what you want" because you haven't exposed yourself to the thing that would make you realize it.

- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

- **tension:** ninguna


### U-026-102
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Stress that can't think clearly makes people adopt assigned goals and run a hamster wheel that digs them deeper into a hole
- **desarrollo:** "Stress can be very good for pushing you towards certain goals", but in the state of not being able to think clearly, "which is 99 of the population", most people "adopt goals that they are told to adopt that are assigned to them" and get on "this hamster wheel": pursue a goal (e.g., a high-paying job, "there's nothing wrong with that"), but not by their own conscious reasoning; reach it, ask "what's next", choose another goal; "none of these are self-generated none of these are actually what they want to do". They're "slowly digging themselves further into a hole rather than being able to climb upwards so they can see the past mistakes".
- **ejemplos:** Pursuing a high-paying job without conscious reasoning.
- **cita:** "most people adopt goals that they are told to adopt that are assigned to them"

## CLÚSTER C-T01b-12: Society is a behavior system (social matrix of goals, three big goals)
- idea: Ya actúas hacia capas de metas inconscientes cada segundo (levantarte, cepillarte, vestirte "so that you aren't seen as an outcast"): "the social matrix of goals creates humans that all operate in a similar fashion" (con otra meta social podríamos ir desnudos tocándonos la cabeza con un palo). "Society is a behavior system" que inyecta tres grandes metas en cuanto comprendes el idioma: escuela, empleo, jubilación. El 99% interpreta cada situación desde ese lente (p. ej. la conversación con el amigo de tus padres) y practica habilidades que lo programan para una vida mediocre sin saberlo; se vuelve "a specialist slave" sin el "deep generalized skill set". La social matrix no es mala (ancla la atención para sobrevivir), pero la pregunta es si solo sobrevives o creas; no abandonas esas metas salvo que alguien desafíe tus creencias.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-018-131
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** The social matrix of goals: layers of unconscious goals (wake, brush teeth, dress) aimed at fitting in
- **desarrollo:** "You are already acting toward layers of unconscious goals every second of the day; they've been conditioned to the point of not requiring conscious thought." You wake up, walk, brush your teeth, "put on your clothes so that you aren't seen as an outcast": skills developed "with the goal of fitting in and surviving in society." "The social matrix of goals creates humans that all operate in a similar fashion. With a slightly different goal of society we could all be walking around naked and communicating with each other by tapping people on the head with a stick." Biological goals clearly influence this. Later: the social matrix "isn't a bad thing, it creates an intellectual structure to anchor our attention so that we can actually survive here," but beyond that: "are you just surviving or are you creating?"
- **ejemplos:** Walking around naked and tapping heads with a stick under different social goals.
- **cita:** "with a slightly different goal of society we could all be walking around naked and communicating with each other by tapping people on the head with a stick"
- **terminos:** social matrix of goals; layers of unconscious goals
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-018-130
- **fuente:** The Daily Routine That Changed My Life (4 Habits Most People Ignore).md, 5:54-6:40 and 13:21, 2023-11-19
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-020-042
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Society is a behavior system that injects three big goals: go to school, get a job, retire
- **desarrollo:** "Society is a behavior system," and right when you learn to comprehend your language it injects three big goals into your mind: go to school, get a job, retire at whatever age you can — "at an old age when you don't really have much time to actually use the rewards." "99% of people" interpret everyday situations only from the lens of those goals and are practicing the skills and programming their minds to live a mediocre life without knowing it. The masses are "shepherded to an unfulfilling life" because the systems composing their mind, identity, perspective and perception become more efficient as they age.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "99% of people are practicing the skills and programming their minds to live a mediocre life without even knowing it"
- **terminos:** behavior system, three big goals
- **origen:** propia

- **desarrollo:** Recap: "your mind is a cybernetic system Society is a behavior system and there are three big goals that they injected into your mind right when you learn to comprehend the language you speak one school 2 job 3 retire." "99% of people are only interpreting everyday situations in a way that leads to those goals." They study what they're told for status and survival, closing their mind to what really matters. "They become a specialist slave who's dependent on those who need that specific knowledge they don't have the Deep generalized skill set that allows them to pursue their own path." "A writer who only learns writing doesn't have a chance at making an independent income." 99% are practicing skills, focusing attention and programming their minds "to live a mediocre life without even knowing it because they failed to question and Revolt."
- **ejemplos:** A writer who only learns writing.
- **cita:** "they become a specialist slave"

### U-021-071
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Talking to your parents' friend through the lens of "school, job, retire at 65"
- **desarrollo:** If your goal is to go to school, get a job and retire at 65, you will interpret conversations through that lens even when they aren't about those things. Talking with a parent's friend, you'll be thinking: "what are they doing for work do I want to end up like that what did they do for schooling what major did they go through how much do they make do they seem like they enjoy their life." That shapes your actions in pursuit of that goal, "but you're probably not going to leave that goal unless they challenge those beliefs of yours."

## CLÚSTER C-T01b-13: Borrowed goals vs self-generated goals (Nature's Compass)
- idea: Si toda acción apunta a una meta, consciente o no, los resultados revelan la meta: la sociedad parece "eager to be overweight, unhealthy, overworked and broke" no porque lo quiera, sino porque no tiene "self-generated goals". Sin meta intrínseca adoptas la claridad de otro (un empleo) y tu atención queda esclavizada en "this meta system called society". La mayoría juega el juego del "useful worker" y no el del "free and fulfilled individual"; por eso intenta un juego nuevo con la mente programada del anterior y fracasa: hay que volverse alguien que vea la posibilidad y tenga la habilidad. "If you don't know what you want, you will be told what you want and you will believe it" (The Art of Focus); la salida de la vida sin sentido es "Nature's Compass": la experimentación, que apunta a lo que tu naturaleza quiere y no a lo que quiere el ego.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-012-003
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** If every action is toward a conscious or unconscious goal, people without self-generated goals end up pursuing outcomes they never chose
- **desarrollo:** The author reasons: if every action is directed toward a goal, conscious or unconscious, then looking at results reveals the goal. Observing society, "it seemed like everyone was eager to be overweight unhealthy overworked and broke" — not because they wanted that, but because they "didn't have self-generated goals that shaped their decision-making". Without self-generated goals, the default (unconscious) goals produce default outcomes. He also asks why people go to the same job that "clearly caused them immense suffering" and how they fail to see the opportunity of the digital world "unfolding right before their eyes".
- **ejemplos:** the grease-leaking breakfast sandwich and dessert drink; people repeating a job that causes suffering
- **cita:** "they didn't have self-generated goals that shaped their decision-making"
- **terminos:** self-generated goals, conscious or unconscious goal
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** idea that behavior is goal-directed
- **fuente:** The Future Of Work Is Play (How To Create A Digital Career).md, 0:55–1:43, 2023-12-31
- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "A huge problem is that people just don't have an intrinsic goal that they're trying to achieve they go and they adopt the goal of someone else because it's Clarity that someone else has created like a job." They give mental energy to it and align their work with it, and eventually "they're so deep in this meta system called society that their attention is just enslaved in that and they don't have any time to go and create something for themselves."

- **cita:** "they're so deep in this meta system called society that their attention is just enslaved in that"
- **terminos:** intrinsic goal; meta system called society; enslaved attention
- **origen:** propia

### U-022-008
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** People pursue unconscious goals installed by parents and society, playing the game of "useful worker" instead of "free and fulfilled individual."
- **desarrollo:** "Most people are pursuing the unconscious goals that their parents and society put into their head. They are playing the game of being a useful worker rather than a free and fulfilled individual." Consequence: people try to play a new game (starting a business, losing weight) "with the programmed mind of an employee or overweight person and wonder why they fail." Conclusion: to achieve anything you must become the person who both (a) sees it as a possibility and (b) has the skill to achieve it. Both conditions are required.
- **ejemplos:** Trying to start a business with the mind of an employee; trying to lose weight with the mind of an overweight person.
- **cita:** "To achieve anything in life you must become the person who both sees that as a possibility and has the skill to achieve it."

## CLÚSTER C-T01b-14: Narrow-minded living (purpose crisis)
- idea: Diagnóstico del estado de quien siguió el camino por defecto: te distrajiste, renunciaste a tus sueños y buscaste seguridad en los de otro (escuela, empleo, cuentas, préstamos estudiantiles), lo que te lleva a un "narrow-minded state of stress and survival": días robóticos, oportunidades escasas. Socialmente es una "purpose crisis": no logramos hacer el trabajo, tareas repetitivas, distracción, falta de claridad, "narrow-minded living" (escuela, empleo, scroll, objetos). La mente está inundada por las metas de padres, amigos, pareja o jefe, y por el riesgo y el dolor de seguir las propias (perder personas y parte de la identidad); la vocación suprimida se expresa en "no tengo tiempo", la vida de los "externally conditioned". Dato retórico: 60.000–80.000 pensamientos al día y 100 negativos por cada uno de una vida mejor. Promesa: el disfrute de la vida se crea deconstruyendo la vida como el juego que es (flow).
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-025-079
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** You're stuck because you got distracted, gave up your dreams and sought security in someone else's: school, job, bills and loans narrow you into stress and survival.
- **desarrollo:** Diagnosis: "you got distracted you lost the battle you gave up your dreams and sought security in someone else's." You didn't pay attention to the path you were on: went to school, got the job, "and now what." The mind is occupied by bills, work you don't care about, student loans "pushed under the rug" and stacking up, lack of time or energy to be healthy, start the business, give life your all. Those thoughts split into a thousand more, walking you into a "narrow-minded state of stress and survival": life loses its zest, days become robotic, opportunities become scarce.
- **ejemplos:** bills; student loans; unwanted work
- **cita:** "you gave up your dreams and sought Security in someone else's"

- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

### U-023-141
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Society's problems: we can't get the work done, a purpose crisis, repetitive tasks, distraction, lack of clarity, narrow-minded living
- **desarrollo:** "The big problem with today's society seems to be that we just can't get the work done". "We're in the middle of a purpose crisis": we lack meaning in careers but don't know what to do; tasks are repetitive and endless "because that's all we're skilled to do"; distractions everywhere pull us from what's important; worst, "we lack clarity on what we want out of life". Dopamine is popular for this reason; it plays a crucial role in flow but goes beyond. Many are stuck in "narrow-minded living": go to school, get a job, scroll phones, chase material objects, never experiencing life's depth. Promise: find enjoyment in the boring, mundane or terrible parts of life, and use flow to achieve meaningful goals "in 6 months rather than 6 years". The result of understanding flow: discovering your life's work, passion for it, and tools to navigate anxiety and overwhelm. "Life enjoyment isn't dictated by randomness or luck, it's created by deconstructing life as the game it is and learning how to play".
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "life enjoyment isn't dictated by Randomness or luck it's created by deconstructing life as the game it is and learning how to play"
- **terminos:** purpose crisis; narrow-minded living; life's work

- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** Life Is A Video Game (Here's How You Win).md, 1:07-2:46, 2024-07-21

## CLÚSTER C-T03a-05: Self-generated vs assigned goals ("if you don't create a purpose, you will be assigned one")
- idea: Siempre persigues metas; si no eliges las tuyas te las asignan padres, escuela, empleador y sociedad. Una meta implica lo que aprendes y los problemas que resuelves: con metas asignadas tu mente queda estrecha, recorres el camino de otros y obtienes sus resultados ("your destiny is decided for you"); además las metas asignadas permanecen fijas y sus medios se optimizan hasta ser reemplazados por tecnología, mientras las autogeneradas evolucionan al lograrse. Los problemas elegidos estrechan la atención y hacen olvidar las preocupaciones; los asignados la esclavizan y las amplifican. Las metas son "the axis of your suffering": eliges cuál sufrir. Para elegir hay que descubrir primero (discovered vs assigned), por ensayo y error.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

- **titulo:** Self-generated goals evolve when achieved; assigned goals stay the same, their means become more efficient over time and get replaced by technology.
- **desarrollo:** Dan Koe: "self-generated goals evolve when achieved assigned goals stay the same and the modes of achieving them become efficient with time and get replaced by technology." This is the mechanism linking assigned goals to replacement: a static goal invites optimization of its means until a machine can do it.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno

### U-013-166
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** A free man acts on his interests; if you don't choose a purpose you'll be assigned one, and your learning is limited to assigned goals
- **desarrollo:** Ericson's second point: "a free man is expected to act on his interests and do many things throughout his life." Dan connects it to his book: "if you don't create a purpose you will be assigned one" (or "if you don't choose a purpose..."). A purpose is a goal; most people don't set and pursue their own goals. Goals are everything — that's how you survive since birth: given goals by parents, teachers, employer, "and that's usually it" for people who can't zoom out to choose long-term goals. Chain: a goal implies learning the knowledge and skills to achieve it -> assigned goals mean you only learn what's needed for those goals -> you explore nothing outside -> you take the same path others took -> you get the results others have -> and 99% aren't satisfied with those results. "If you don't choose your own goal you do not choose what you learn or what problems you solve; your destiny is decided for you, because the only potential you know is the one you were assigned."
- **ejemplos:** Parents, teachers, employer as goal assigners.
- **cita:** "if you don't choose your own goal you do not choose what you learn or what problems you solve your destiny is decided for you"
- **terminos:** if you don't choose a purpose you will be assigned one, self-generated goals
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Devon Ericson
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-013-165
- **fuente:** The Future-Proof Skill Stack (How Average People Become Millionaires).md, 2:16-3:46, 2024-03-24
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-027-204

- **titulo:** If you don't create a purpose, you will be assigned one; how to figure out what you want is trial and error
- **desarrollo:** You need your own goals, not assigned ones; "if you don't create a purpose you will be assigned one," and only self-created goals keep you from being susceptible to goals society tries to assign "at every waking second." Get absolutely fed up with where you are, reject everything you thought true, start from scratch. How to figure out what you want: "it's trial and error"—mistakes give direction. Latching onto someone else's prescription, you expect their results but often won't get them—unless the results are mediocre and easy, like a job or degree everyone gets, which isn't fulfilling because you're not unique. You know how to pave the way; you're just afraid to start and enter the unknown.
- **ejemplos:** job, degree.
- **cita:** "if you don't create a purpose you will be assigned one"
- **terminos:** trial and error; assigned goals
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

### U-020-125
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Discovered vs. assigned (chosen vs. assigned) goals — once you taste a new path, your potential keeps nagging
- **desarrollo:** Life passes you by because you're exposed to the same information daily; it hasn't changed much since you achieved society's goals. When you start a new path it compounds: conversations with friends shift; search history goes from "something you probably shouldn't be searching" to "best business models of 2024"; you get "a little taste of what life could be." You'll probably fail the first and second time, but "your potential will always be there even if you quit it won't stop nagging you there's no going back." Why: the goals your mind operates on have fundamentally changed — from what parents, teachers and society assigned to something you chose through discovery and reading. "That's the entire difference is discovered versus assigned or chosen versus assigned but you have to discover it first in order to choose it." So: you need a new goal and to self-educate to reach it — "that simple process alone will be responsible for much of the joy in your life." "True education is about curiosity and discovery not memorization and Conformity."
- **ejemplos:** Search history change.
- **cita:** "you have to discover it first in order to choose it"
- **terminos:** discovered vs. assigned, chosen vs. assigned

- **nivel:** fundamental

## CLÚSTER C-T04-26: Everything is goal-oriented: unconscious assigned goals vs conscious self-generated goals
- idea: Toda acción está dirigida a una meta, la mayoría de las veces de forma inconsciente: las 8–10 horas de pantalla del gamer son el infierno del culturista porque no es su meta. "Your mind is the operating system for reality. That system is composed of goals", y para la mayoría esas metas fueron programadas "like lines of code in their psyche" (escuela, empleo, ofenderse, hacerse la víctima, jubilarse a los 65). Sin metas propias conscientes, cada acción carece de sentido y se opera desde las metas que la sociedad proyectó (empleos de corto plazo, sexo superficial, "low conscious problems"), que igualmente hay que resolver, pero con visión de largo plazo y aprendiendo del fracaso ("failures are the foundation for your quality of life"). Bajo la inseguridad psicológica solo hay tres opciones: fijarse en el futuro predecible (ansiedad), en el pasado familiar (aburrimiento), ambas adormecidas con distracción, o fijar una meta nueva y navegar lo desconocido ("if you don't have a goal the only option is to get distracted"). Cuando lo externo se alinea con lo interno, con metas autocreadas, "the unconscious sends resources". Para volverse más inteligente: rechazar el camino conocido, fijar metas más altas, abrazar el caos y ser un "deep generalist".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: la misma tesis del sistema operativo se reformula en 2025-12 (U-025-154) bajo el marco de la teleología, que en 2024-11 (U-021-062) no aparecía.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-017-117
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Everything is goal-oriented: without conscious self-generated goals you operate from unconscious goals society projected onto you.
- **desarrollo:** Without a vision, "every single one of your actions are meaningless," and you view life "from a lens of unconscious goals because everything is goal oriented." If you don't have your own, you operate from goals society projected on you — a better short-term job, quick superficial sex, "every other low conscious problem" people focus on solving that never gets them anywhere. Nuance: you do have to solve these problems, but from a long-term standpoint. E.g., even if you just want sex: expose yourself to the experience, fail a few times — "failures are the foundation for your quality of life" — learn by exposure to reality; "don't just sit around mentally masturbating over things"; reap lessons and channel failures toward your goals.
- **ejemplos:** Short-term job; dating failures.
- **cita:** "failures are the foundation for your quality of life"
- **terminos:** unconscious goals, conscious self-generated goals, low conscious problem
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-116
- **fuente:** Focus Is A Superpower (Take Back Control Of Your Life).md, 15:04–15:48, 2023-07-11
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-018-081
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Every action works toward a goal, mostly unconsciously: the gamer's 8-10 hours on screen is the bodybuilder's living hell
- **desarrollo:** The gamer can sit in front of a screen 8 to 10 hours a day "and like their life"; a bodybuilder "would see that as a living hell" because they've studied bodybuilding and nutrition enough to know it's "the worst thing that they could do for their health"; if their goal is health and bodybuilding "they're not going to do that because it's not their goal." "You're working towards goals with every single action you take; much of the time that's just unconscious." When you make goals conscious and invest energy into a new goal for who you want to become, "it's going to be difficult at first, but you're eventually going to create the identity that you want to create, and those actions are going to come naturally."
- **ejemplos:** Gamer vs. bodybuilder on 8-10 hours of screen time.
- **cita:** "you're working towards goals with every single action you take much of the time that's just unconscious"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-018-080

### U-025-154
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Teleology: goals determine how you see the world and what counts as success; your mind is an operating system made of goals, mostly programmed by others — reject the known path to become intelligent.
- **desarrollo:** He speaks "from the lens of teleology or the Greek cosmos with a K": everything serves a purpose, part of a greater whole. Goals determine how you see the world and what you consider success or failure. You can try to enjoy the process, but "if you pursue the wrong goal, you will not enjoy it." "Your mind is the operating system for reality. That system is composed of goals." For most, goals are assigned, "programmed like lines of code in your psyche": go to school, get a job, get offended, play victim, retire at 65 — a known path that doesn't work. To become intelligent: reject the known path, dive into the unknown, set new higher goals to expand your mind, embrace the chaos, allow growth; study generalized principles of nature and become "a deep generalist."
- **ejemplos:** school -> job -> retire at 65
- **cita:** "Your mind is the operating system for reality. That system is composed of goals."

### U-006-159
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** The three options under psychological insecurity: fixate on the predictable future (anxiety), the familiar past (boredom) — both numbed with distraction — or set a new goal and navigate the unknown.
- **desarrollo:** Dan Koe: for the 99% in careers or life directions "that lead to this psychological insecurity that you think is security," the only options are: (1) "focus on the predictable future and get anxious so you numb your mind with distractions"; (2) "focus on the familiar past and get bored so again you numb your mind with distractions"; (3) "set a new goal so you can finally take control of your life and start navigating the unknown the only place you can learn grow and expand." "If you don't have a goal the only option is to get distracted."

- **cita:** "if you don't have a goal the only option is to get distracted"

### U-027-002
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** When the external aligns with the internal, the unconscious sends resources to fuel the lifestyle you are building
- **desarrollo:** The counterpart to unconscious misalignment: when your goals—"the ones that you create"—align with who you really are deep down, "the unconscious sends resources to help fuel that lifestyle that you are trying to build." The condition is that the goals are self-created (intrinsic), not adopted from the company, school or culture. Koe offers multiple framings ("what you truly enjoy", "what aligns with you the most", "what speaks to you the most") and says he prefers to keep an open mind toward the "spiritual way of saying these things" instead of dismissing it as "hippie or new age", because the only way to connect it to something you truly understand is to have an open mind to everything—not only what he says but what everyone says.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno

- **terminos:** external aligns with the internal; the unconscious; intrinsic goals

# SECCIÓN 1.4 — Imitar es inevitable: NPC o main character


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-11: Create or be created
- idea: "We're in a world where it's create or be created": si no moldeas tu mente para crear tu propio juego, ya fue moldeada para jugar los "external status games" de la sociedad (universidad, empleo, cualquier estructura que diseñó otro). Eso trae sufrimiento innecesario cuando no encaja con tus metas. "There's no such thing as doing nothing with life": o haces lo que quieres o lo que otros quieren, y lo segundo vuelve la vida cada vez más mecánica. Por la misma lógica, quien no crea su propio producto es "assigned" a vender el de otros y se vuelve "a puppet to society". La mente desea orden y certeza, por eso el 99% acude al camino seguro. El "sovereign living" es difícil e incierto, pero es la manera de salir de la Matrix.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-023-229 <= U-003-020 | "create or be created" con texto casi literal; U-003-020 es la reaparición en la compilación de 2024; U-023-229 (original de 2022) es más completo porque agrega la certeza y el sovereign living

### U-023-229
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Create or be created: if you don't mold your mind to create your own game, it's molded to play society's external status games; masses flock to certainty
- **desarrollo:** "We're in a world where it's create or be created". If you can't mold your mind to create your own reality/game, "your mind will be molded, and it already has been molded, to play external status games that society has created for you": going to college, getting a job, or another structured approach someone else laid out, which may not fit you and leads to unnecessary suffering because you follow steps that may not align with your personal goals. "Sovereign living or autonomous living", building your own way of survival, is difficult and uncertain, and "the mind craves order, it craves certainty", so where there's an easier, more certain, secure path, "that's where 99% of people flock". The video's purpose is awareness for those who share his values: build your own thing, be independent, "break free of the Matrix if you want to frame it that way", live enjoyably on your own terms.
- **ejemplos:** College, job.
- **cita:** "we're in a world where it's create or be created"
- **terminos:** create or be created; external status games; sovereign living; autonomous living
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

## CLÚSTER C-T01b-11: NPC vs main character
- idea: Imitas desde que naciste; la imitación lleva a dos caminos: NPC o main character; la diferencia está en imitar de forma consciente. En los juegos el NPC está programado y da guiones; en la vida es "a slave to their programming": nunca cuestiona creencias proyectadas y aceptadas "without a fight", sigue escuela–empleo–jubilación a los 65 y su vida se vuelve "boring, mechanical and animalistic". Los NPC (aldeanos, entorno, jefes) mantienen la estructura del juego para los jugadores; quienes cuestionan el sistema y ven el panorama completo pueden jugarlo. Las creencias condicionadas automatizan pensamientos y te vuelven "a puppet... an emotional light switch". Por condicionamiento social la mayoría sigue siendo NPC ("groundless but necessary, a tool not a master"); cada acción de un personaje consciente o inconsciente contribuye al futuro.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-023-232 <= U-003-026 | mismo pasaje sobre los NPC (aldeanos, entorno, jefes que mantienen la estructura del juego) de "How I Turned My Life Into A Video Game" (2022), repetido en la compilación "Get Ahead Of 99% Of People With Deep Work & Monk Mode" (2024)

This can lead down two paths: the path of the NPC (non-player character) and the path of the main character. The difference is not whether you imitate but whether you do it consciously (developed later as "intelligent imitation").

### U-023-081

- **titulo:** The NPC: a person who is a slave to their programming, never questioning beliefs projected onto them
- **desarrollo:** In a game, an NPC is anyone programmed to execute specific things, not a player character requiring conscious input; you interact with them and they have pre-programmed beliefs, ideas or scripts they say to help the conscious individual play the game better. In real life, NPCs are "the people that are a slave to their programming": they never question their beliefs, the thoughts in their head or what they're saying; don't become aware of what they're doing and its impact on their own and others' lives. Their beliefs were projected onto them and "accepted without a fight". They never seek to change the default path — school, job, retire at 65 are their only big goals. They never branch into the unknown, so life becomes "boring, mechanical and animalistic"; they never discover new potentials, and if they do, it scares them back into their comfortable hole.
- **ejemplos:** Video game NPCs with scripted lines.

## CLÚSTER C-T02a-38: Archetypes and metatypes
- idea: Un arquetipo es un patrón de personas en un nivel dentro de un cuadrante. Un metatype es la síntesis de los cuatro arquetipos de una persona. Las progresiones principales son NPC → player → creator (mente), incel → Chad → sigma (cuerpo), religion → atheism → mysticism (espíritu) y job → career → calling (vocación), y el autor da listas de arquetipos posibles por nivel en cada cuadrante, como ejemplos ilustrativos. El ejemplo de metatype "the outlier" (Human 2.0 global) es alguien que se alejó de lo convencional sin rebelarse y opera desde una lente contracultural. La lógica para agregar puntos no se explica.
- nivel: complementario

- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

## CLÚSTER C-T02b-10: Intelligent imitation (create or be created)
- idea: Nadie escapa del condicionamiento, e imitar es necesario porque así se aprende. Según Dan, lo que separa al NPC del main character es imitar de forma consciente, con filtro y autorreflexión, y elegir los propios inputs: "create or be created". Propone cuatro pasos: (1) crear una perspectiva en la que crecer (zoom out hacia el ideal future self o hacia el universo), (2) elegir de 3 a 5 mentores, (3) actuar como un mad scientist, (4) crear o ser creado. El condicionamiento solo es dañino cuando la conducta no lleva a la vida que pide tu "core". Dan polemiza con los "authenticity bros".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: tensión entre autenticidad e imitación (ver Evolución)
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-023-092
- **tipo:** término-acuñado
- **titulo:** Intelligent imitation: copying consciously, with a filter and self-reflection
- **desarrollo:** "Becoming the main character through what I call intelligent imitation". Since you've copied people since birth, you need to copy/imitate, because that's how you learn: you see someone do it, try it, practice, get better. But do it "with a conscious mind": with a filter and self-reflection so you aren't heading in the wrong direction. To condition your mind for success: set new goals for a higher quality of life; immerse yourself in information that conditions a new perspective; make progress and notice the positive outcome on your life. The NPC is unconscious of how they imitate; the main character is aware of how they imitate others and what they do with the information (creating with it? doing good with it?), reflects on decisions, sees what went right or wrong, and corrects their path. Four steps follow: (1) create a perspective you can grow into, (2) choose 3-5 mentors, (3) become a mad scientist, (4) create or be created.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "the main character is someone who is conscious or aware of how they are imitating other people and what they are doing with that information"
- **terminos:** intelligent imitation; filter; self-reflection
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-080, U-023-091
- **fuente:** Most People Are NPCs (Don't Waste Your Life On Autopilot).md, 8:13-8:58, 12:35, 2023-05-08
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-023-098
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Intelligent imitation step 1: create a perspective you can grow into by expanding to your ideal future self or to the universe
- **desarrollo:** "One of the most impactful lessons": think of perspective as an expanding and contracting thing that determines open- vs closed-mindedness, open vs narrow focus, stressful vs creative and happy. The highest perspective you can adopt is that of God, the universe, "the Tao" (transcribed "tail"), "what everything is". His favorite two most conducive expansions: (1) your ideal future self; (2) the universe. When facing a problem, become conscious of it, zoom out, open your perspective, and train your mind to hold a wider perspective for longer. Practice today: when stressed or negative, zoom out — first, how would your ideal self (10-20 years from now) interpret it; second, the universe: "zoom out beyond everything, stop caring and see what happens".
- **ejemplos:** A 16-year-old crying after a breakup: zoom out to the universe — in a thousand years I'll be dead, nobody will know me; in a year I won't care, I'll be in another grade, maybe another state; there are billions of other women. In business, losing $100,000 "is a problem, that sucks", but what can you do — can you make it back?
- **cita:** "you zoom out you open your perspective and you train your mind to do this so that you can hold a wider perspective for a longer period of time"
- **terminos:** perspective you can grow into; zoom out; ideal future self; open focus; narrow focus
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-083
- **fuente:** Most People Are NPCs (Don't Waste Your Life On Autopilot).md, 12:35-15:38, 2023-05-08
- **tension:** ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "The primary difference between the NPC and the main character is creation". The NPC is created through years of conditioning; the main character creates themselves through years of conscious conditioning: they choose their inputs. "Nobody escapes conditioning, it's happening all around you, this video is conditioning you". "The modern world is create or be created: you have to create yourself or you will be created, you have to create or you will be given something created to give". "The choice is yours literally every millisecond of the day".

- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Imitation is necessary; conditioning becomes bad only when behavior isn't conducive to the life your core is calling for
- **desarrollo:** The conditioning process isn't bad; "imitation is necessary". Against "authenticity bros" who claim they don't imitate: you walk on two feet, speak English and live in a house — "you imitate because you learn". If you want to be "super authentic", start walking on four legs and see whether you're cast out of your group and whether you can survive. Conditioning becomes bad "when your behavior isn't conducive to the life that your core or that voice inside you is calling out for" — the whisper that "you're meant for more", felt especially in a fluorescent-lit cubicle.

## CLÚSTER C-T07a-26: Intelligent imitation
- idea: "Success is a game of intelligent imitation" (término que el autor dice haber inventado): la imitación, el condicionamiento o la programación no se pueden evitar porque la mente procesa la información quiera o no; por eso hay que hacerlo conscientemente. Se aprende de otros y luego se transmite: quienes te enseñan tu interés están haciendo un negocio, y con la misma pericia tú también puedes. Proceso: elegir 3–5 mentores ("you become what you consume, so consume in accordance with who you want to become") con una voz congruente para ti; tomar de ellos también producto, tono, colores y diseño; la combinación es única y por eso auténtica, porque cada persona piensa en 3–5 nombres distintos. Copiar palabra por palabra fracasa (los imitadores que le escriben por DM); hay que tomar las ideas, contemplarlas "in the chaos of your head" y volver con algo nuevo. Si no sabes qué proyecto crear, emula lo que otros han hecho (un newsletter, un lote de tweets, un tutorial de Photoshop).
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: renumeración del proceso: "choose 3–5 mentors" es el paso 1 en 2022 y el paso 2 en 2023, donde se añade "become a mad scientist" como paso 3 (C-T07a-24).

## CLÚSTER C-T08-39: Intelligent imitation
- idea: "The key to initial success in most areas of life is intelligent imitation": todos imitamos inconscientemente; hacerlo de forma consciente e inteligente, imitando a quienes quieres parecerte, aprovecha los beneficios de encajar sin volverse esclavo de los sistemas. Si todos lo hacen no está saturado sino que es rentable: haz lo mismo, un poco más único. Con Wilson Mizner (y "steal like an artist"), tomar de un escritor es plagio y de muchos es investigación. Método: anotar lo que te gusta y lo que no de otros y llenar el hueco (destilar The Power of Now para quien lo encuentra demasiado etéreo), aplicable a voz, colores y detalles de la web; construir una "tribe of mentors" y sumergirte en su obra hasta que el subconsciente produzca ideas originales. Advertencia: destacar en un mundo de productos copy-paste lleva tiempo y experiencia; la conciencia es solo el punto de partida.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: la imitación es el inicio y la síntesis el objetivo de largo plazo (C-T08-40). Ver tensión "exclusively consume" vs diversidad de fuentes en "Evolución y tensiones".

### U-009-236
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** Intelligent imitation is the key to initial success; if everyone is doing it, it's profitable, not saturated — do it a little more uniquely
- **desarrollo:** "The key to initial success in most areas of life is intelligent imitation." We all imitate others growing up, but often unconsciously, ending up where we don't like. That doesn't make imitation useless as a survival strategy: if you do it "consciously, intelligently," imitating the people you want to become like, you're more likely to become like them. People overcomplicate business thinking "if everyone else is doing it, it's saturated. No, not even close. If everyone else is doing it that means it's profitable, it means people actually want it." Your job: "do the same thing but make it just a little bit more unique."

Uno es un "perspective vessel" que interpreta la información a su manera y la empaqueta según la meta propia o de la audiencia. Prácticas concretas: escribir con la voz del autor favorito, usar el tema de una cuenta como inspiración, tomar los problemas que ataca la landing de un competidor, deconstruir por qué algo se volvió viral y si dio dinero, mirar qué vende todo el mundo, y reescribir una idea de memoria sin mirar el texto.

Born across the world, you'd speak a different language. You're a copycat by nature: you see something, interpret it, and either discard or adopt it. # SECCIÓN 1.5 — El entorno informativo: algoritmos, memes y contenido entrópico


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-27: The epistemic commons (information environment and conditioning)
- idea: La información que consumes moldea tu vida: información → condicionamiento → identidad → conducta → trayectoria ("you think something as simple as reading a little social media post doesn't determine where you end up in life, you were wrong"). Los "epistemic commons" (concepto de Daniel Schmachtenberger) son el suministro de agua de la información, y se están envenenando si lo que se publica daña más de lo que ayuda. También importa la forma: el contenido corto o largo entrena la capacidad de atención, la tolerancia a la complejidad, la capacidad de sostener contradicciones y el matiz. Antes de resolver cualquier amenaza civilizatoria se necesita una población capaz de entenderla. Los algoritmos hacen el condicionamiento "10 times worse" porque te dan lo que tu meta pide, incluida la mediocridad. Con acceso universal y una mayoría no desarrollada, internet se llena de "level one thinking". Respuesta práctica, ya en 2022: si todos consumen la misma información y llegan a un resultado que no quieres, no la consumas ("contrarian inputs"). Los ensayos quizá sean una de las últimas formas que desarrollan la capacidad de dar sentido a la realidad.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: de una respuesta individual de consumo (2022–2023) a un marco civilizatorio (2025–2026). Ver E-06.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-014-114
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** Layer 1: the epistemic commons — the water supply for information — is being poisoned
- **desarrollo:** The epistemic commons: "the water supply, but for information" — social media, news, any public information resource. Example: most people watch the news to stay informed, but they're getting complacent, not thinking; their lives aren't changing for the better; more people are becoming jaded, polarized, violent. Whenever anyone posts on social media, makes a TV show or movie, or publishes music on Spotify, the epistemic commons/public information environment grows. It's complex and requires systems thinking, but if the content published in public hurts more than it helps and isn't counterbalanced by content that helps, "our intellectual water source becomes contaminated."
- **ejemplos:** News watchers becoming jaded/polarized; posts, TV shows, Spotify music adding to the commons.
- **cita:** "you can think of it as the water supply, but for information"
- **terminos:** epistemic commons, mental water supply, intellectual water source, public information environment
- **origen:** adaptada-de:Daniel Schmachtenberger (the term is presented within his framework; author's water analogy)
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-014-113
- **fuente:** I'm begging you to start writing essays (even if you hate writing).md, 3:04-3:58, 2026-04-05
- **tension:** ninguna

Identity, shaped by consumed information, influences behavior and thus life trajectory — "it determines the outcome of your life." "You think something as simple as reading a little social media post doesn't determine where you end up in life, you were wrong."

### U-014-116
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** The form of information trains attention span, tolerance for complexity, holding contradictions and nuance
- **desarrollo:** Beyond content, the form of what you consume (short-form tweet vs long-form books, articles, podcasts) trains your attention span, tolerance for complexity, ability to hold contradictions and capacity for nuance — which is why you learn: to equip yourself with knowledge and cognitive capacity to get what you want. At the civilizational level: before solving any civilizational threat (climate, governance, AI alignment, public health), you need a population capable of understanding the problem coherently; "99% of people don't even know what these problems entail because they're happy drooling over the cat video." Takeaway question: does what you consume or create lead to a beneficial behavior change in yourself and others, or are you unconsciously soaking in information that silently worsens your life and poisons the environment through what you contribute?

### U-007-095
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Contrarian inputs: if everyone consumes the same information and it leads to an outcome you don't want, don't consume it
- **desarrollo:** If everyone did something, Dan wanted the opposite: everyone wanted to retire at 65, he wanted to retire at 30; everyone watched TV at night, he went to the gym or watched educational YouTube to increase his skill set so he wouldn't get stuck in a low-skill labor job (which are consistently being phased out). Watching the news, "keeping up with the Kardashians", staying informed on everything didn't appeal; he wanted to read books and develop skills. The connection he made: if everyone consumes the same information and it leads to a specific life outcome, why would he consume that information?
- **ejemplos:** retire at 65 vs 30; TV vs gym or educational YouTube; news and Kardashians vs books.
- **cita:** "if everyone is consuming the same information and it's leading to this specific outcome in life then why would i consume that specific information"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-007-094
- **fuente:** 7 Lessons From 7 Failed Online Businesses (And Making 7 Figures).md, 1:01-1:57, 2022-06-18

## CLÚSTER C-T01b-03: Social media and AI amplify unconsciousness
- idea: Capa 5 de la social matrix: el modelo de propaganda de Herman y Chomsky (cinco filtros: propiedad corporativa, publicidad, fuentes de élite, campañas de "flak", ideología dominante) describe la propaganda tradicional; redes sociales e IA la llevan a "an entirely new level". Las redes son "a mimetic desire amplification machine": los algoritmos dicen mostrarte lo que quieres, pero "they program you to want what others appear to have" (Peter Thiel invirtió en Facebook por entender la mimética). Contrapunto 2023: las redes no son netamente negativas; documentan "the collective mind", subimos nuestras mentes a internet y la pantalla es la última barrera; tienes el conocimiento del mundo a mano para aprender y crear un negocio.

## CLÚSTER C-T04-15: Content and consciousness: structured vs entropic content
- idea: "There is content and there is consciousness": la consciencia es el campo sensorial y el contenido es aquello con lo que interactuamos; "the structure of that content dictates the structure of our consciousness". Como la mente tiende al desorden, necesita información estructurada y comprendida para ordenarse ("people hate what they don't understand", con la salvedad de que no todo encaja). El contenido rápido y superficial es "entropic": cuanto más se consume, más caótica la mente (el "WALL-E citizen", 50 posts leídos en conjunto, la fiesta en la cabeza que deja el "mental room" lleno de vasos rojos); la gratificación instantánea es "inherently entropic" y produce una sociedad de "one marshmallow thinking" sin hilo conductor. No es el formato: el short-form de James Clear, Naval o Huberman es "syntropic" porque apunta a un fin con sentido. En 2026 lo formula como fast content (preempaquetado, deja "informed but empty") vs slow content (ensayos que obligan a escritor y lector a ordenar la consciencia).
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: de "structured content orders consciousness" (2022-12) a la crítica del contenido corto entrópico (2025), matizada con la excepción "syntropic" (2025-09), y al par fast/slow content a favor del ensayo (2026-04).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-007-162 <= U-001-100 | mismo pasaje "content and consciousness" de 2022-12 reeditado en la compilación de 2024-02

### U-007-162
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** The mind tends toward disorder (entropy); structured content orders consciousness
- **desarrollo:** The mind is a system and systems tend toward disorder because of entropy. To maintain order we must consume or receive structured, ordered information we understand. People hate what they don't understand (he admits not everything fits this): their mind isn't ordered because they don't understand the information; they're missing a side of the argument. In communication terms there is "content and there is consciousness": consciousness is our senses or sensory field; content is what we interact with and receive information from. The structure of content dictates the structure of our consciousness, ordering it "to the point of just feeling good".
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "the structure of that content dictates the structure of our consciousness"
- **terminos:** entropy; content and consciousness; order
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** avanzado
- **prerrequisitos:** U-007-161
- **fuente:** You Have A $100,000 Product In Your Head (One Person Business Series).md, 2:28-3:39, 2022-12-04
- **tension:** ninguna

Consciousness is our senses/sensory field; content is what we interact with and receive information from. No long-term sense-making — useless ideas without a goal or vision to apply them. Digital food doesn't energize or build muscle; it makes you sluggish. Being a "WALL-E citizen" drooling over a curated algorithm keeps you in stress and survival; fear and desire pull you away from the present and "psychic entropy or disorder in the mind increases". What seemed one harmless post was actually 50 posts read in unison that silently induce chaos. You regress or stay the same, locked into "a paradigm of political ideologies and static opinions" not conducive to anything worthwhile — and you don't care or don't realize, or realize and forget. He admits it happens to him too.

That's why they feel informed, but empty." ## CLÚSTER C-T06-07: The fast food of socialization (evolutionary mismatch and digital fat, sugar and salt)
- idea: "Social media is the fast food of socialization, porn is the fast food of intimacy, video games are the fast food of achievement, Netflix is the fast food of entertainment." El argumento es un desajuste evolutivo: nuestros circuitos de recompensa se formaron durante unos 2 millones de años (300.000 como Homo sapiens) para buscar grasa, azúcar y sal escasos, y la era industrial (unos 300 años, "1/1,000th of human evolution") los volvió abundantes. Las cadenas de comida rápida probaron combinaciones hasta enganchar; las empresas de internet hicieron lo mismo al adoptar el modelo publicitario y descubrir que los titulares polarizantes retienen más atención: "that's how the For You page was born". El equivalente digital es "digital fat, sugar and salt": fat = fearmongering, sugar = promesas rápidas y deseables, salt = opiniones absolutistas. El resultado es "processed content" y obesidad mental: dinero, sexo, conocimiento, comida y entretenimiento rápidos, "a ticking time bomb".
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna; el argumento se repite con la misma estructura en 2024 y 2025 (en 2025 se condensa en una sola cadena, U-015-159).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): FUSION: U-018-019 <= U-015-158 | las mismas cuatro analogías de "fast food of…" repetidas casi literalmente en un video de 2025; U-015-158 solo añade el encuadre "could lead to the end of the world"

### U-018-019
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** Modern technologies as "fast food" of basic human needs
- **desarrollo:** Dan opens with a chain of parallels: "social media is the fast food of socialization, porn is the fast food of intimacy, video games are the fast food of achievement, Netflix is the fast food of entertainment." Fast food itself is "a combination of fat, sugar and salt, ingredients that used to be incredibly scarce," combined and tested in infinite different ways "to see which keeps consumers coming back for more." The metaphor frames the whole video: each technology delivers a cheap, engineered version of something humans needed and that used to be scarce, and companies iterate on combinations to maximize repeat consumption.
- **ejemplos:** Social media/socialization, porn/intimacy, video games/achievement, Netflix/entertainment.
- **cita:** "social media is the fast food of socialization porn is the fast food of intimacy video games are the fast food of achievement Netflix is the fast food of entertainment"
- **terminos:** fast food of socialization/intimacy/achievement/entertainment; cheap dopamine epidemic
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** The Cheap Dopamine Epidemic Stop Ruining Your Life.md, 0:00, 2024-11-24
- **tension:** ninguna

Porn is the fast food of intimacy. Video games are the fast food of achievement. And Netflix is the fast food of entertainment." Each delivers a hyper-concentrated version of something our reward circuits evolved to seek (developed in the next unit).
- **ejemplos:** The four pairings.
- **cita:** "social media is the fast food of socialization"

- **desarrollo:** Dan asks the viewer to "take note": the "homogeneous species" (as transcribed; i.e., the human genus) emerged around 2 million years ago, homo sapiens around 300,000 years ago, and the Industrial Age started about 300 years ago, "just a tiny fraction of what we are wired for." Human life spent most of its time in foraging, horticultural and agrarian societies "where none of what we had now exists," and "that is very important to this whole dopamine thing": the industrial and informational ages "change everything because we're not wired for that."

### U-018-024
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** From scarcity to abundance: industrial wealth, assembly lines, fast food, TV, internet, and the race to the top
- **desarrollo:** Historical chain: as the Industrial Age progressed, fat, sugar and salt became less scarce (salt traded in coastal areas, fat and sugar produced rapidly). Between 1920 and 1950 assembly lines and 9-to-5 jobs became a thing ("thanks Henry Ford"), shaping the workforce we know today. Humans had more money and potential; with more resources and trade, they became wealthier. After 1950 fast food chains started popping up. Then the information age: the Golden Age of Television spread information; later the internet made information "grow and spread like wildfire"; then Google, Facebook and others competed "in a race to the top." These "weren't all bad at first," but "the desire for power caused technology to evolve in a way towards poor incentives," because everything was new, happening fast, never done before, "everyone was trying to win the game."
- **ejemplos:** Henry Ford's assembly lines; fast food chains after 1950; Golden Age of Television; Google, Facebook.
- **cita:** "the desire for power caused technology to evolve in a way towards poor incentives towards power"
- **terminos:** race to the top; poor incentives
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-018-023
- **fuente:** The Cheap Dopamine Epidemic Stop Ruining Your Life.md, 3:46-4:39, 2024-11-24
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-018-025
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Why fat, sugar and salt hook us: scarce survival resources spike dopamine, and companies test combinations
- **desarrollo:** Humans aren't adapted to have fat, sugar and salt in excess; we're adapted for survival. Scarce ingredients "spurt dopamine into our brain because we need to remember them for survival, we need to see them as important." Once companies discovered this mechanism they could test fat, sugar and salt "in infinite different ways to see which kept the customers hooked." Example: fast food restaurants with "quadruple patty burgers and quesa chalupa burrito tacos," constantly releasing and promoting new combinations so you keep going back to Taco Bell, McDonald's, Carl's Jr.
- **ejemplos:** Quadruple patty burgers; Taco Bell, McDonald's, Carl's Jr. product combinations.
- **cita:** "scarce ingredients like fat sugar and salt spurt dopamine into our brain because we need to remember them for survival"
- **terminos:** fat, sugar and salt

- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-018-019
- **fuente:** The Cheap Dopamine Epidemic Stop Ruining Your Life.md, 4:39-5:35, 2024-11-24
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-015-159
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Evolutionary mismatch chain: hunter-gatherer reward circuits → industrial abundance → fast food → TV → internet ad model → For You page
- **desarrollo:** The chain, in order: (1) Our brains evolved reward circuits for a hunter-gatherer environment: seeking scarce fat, sugar, salt raised dopamine to signal survival importance. (2) Timeline: the "homogeneous species" (Homo genus) emerged ~2 million years ago, Homo sapiens ~300,000 years ago, the industrial age ~300 years ago — "1/1,000th of human evolution." We live in a technological time with ancestral brains. (3) The industrial age made fat, sugar, salt mass-manufactured and distributed. (4) 1950s: fast food chains and the golden age of television (mass spread of information). (5) The internet: incredible at first, then profit-driven companies like Google and Facebook competed in "a race to the top." (6) Fast food companies had already tested combinations of fat, sugar and salt to maximize pleasure; internet companies could likewise study the reward mechanism, leading them to adopt the advertising model. (7) They found the most polarizing or inflammatory headlines/content captured and held the most attention, so they prioritized it and served more of what people liked — "that's how the For You page was born."
- **ejemplos:** Fat/sugar/salt; Google, Facebook; For You page.
- **cita:** "That's 1/ 1,000th of human evolution"
- **terminos:** reward circuits, race to the top, For You page
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-015-158
- **fuente:** The Death Of Social Media (& The Future Of Content Creation).md, 1:28-3:22, 2025-09-07
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-018-026
- **tipo:** término-acuñado
- **titulo:** "Digital fat, sugar and salt": fearmongering, quick desirable promises, absolutistic takes
- **desarrollo:** The same mechanism is "reflected in the digital world at a much faster rate because it's digital, it's not physical." When internet companies adopted an advertising model for growth and monetization, they realized "inflammatory headlines, negativity and things like that led to capturing and retaining the most attention," so they served users more of what they paid attention to: "in comes the For You page, the thing that you're stuck scrolling all day." Mapping: fat = fearmongering; sugar = "quick and desirable promises"; salt = "absolutistic takes" ("in many cases, not all cases"). Companies test combinations and "the algorithm feeds us as much as we want until we're mentally obese." Fear-induced content spurts dopamine and "narrows our thinking because we need the information for later survival." Result: "quick money, quick sex, quick knowledge, quick food, quick entertainment, a ticking time bomb that feels good at first but feels terrible later."
- **ejemplos:** The For You page; inflammatory headlines.
- **cita:** "we can think of fat as fearmongering sugar as quick and desirable promises and salt as absolutistic takes"
- **terminos:** digital fat sugar and salt; mentally obese; For You page
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-018-025
- **fuente:** The Cheap Dopamine Epidemic Stop Ruining Your Life.md, 5:35-6:30, 2024-11-24
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-012-120
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** Processed content as digital fat, sugar and salt — the birth of the For You page
- **desarrollo:** Chain: (1) Fast food is addictive through the combination of fat, sugar and salt — scarce resources humans aren't adapted to have in abundance; when found, they spurt dopamine to signal survival aid. (2) Companies discovered this mechanism and tested combinations that kept consumers coming back. (3) Internet companies did the same: they adopted an advertising model and found polarizing, inflammatory headlines captured the most attention. (4) They served more of what users liked on an instantly accessible screen — "the birth of the For You page". (5) So there's processed food and "processed content": "digital fat sugar and salt", cheap dopamine, instant gratification, entropy.
- **ejemplos:** fast food; inflammatory headlines; For You page
- **cita:** "there's processed food and there's also processed content digital fat sugar and salt"
- **terminos:** processed content, digital fat sugar and salt, For You page, cheap dopamine

- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T06-08: The digital dark ages (attention economy, incentives and personal responsibility)
- idea: Vivimos en "the digital dark ages": una era de distracción, mente cerrada y cheap dopamine, análoga a la Edad Media antes del Renacimiento, en la que se perdió la confianza en los sistemas de creencias que daban certeza. Las empresas de comida rápida, streaming, gaming y redes invierten cientos de miles de millones en "dopamine driven feedback loops" (Facebook, más de 20.000 millones al año en I+D), y el "digital slot machine" roba la atención "10 seconds at a time". El tiempo de pantalla medio en EE. UU. supera las 7 horas y la IA multiplicará el contenido adictivo. Lo que parece "tu interés" al hacer scroll suele ser un interés adictivo o asignado que sirve a las corporaciones. Matiz importante: darse un gusto no es el problema; el problema es la falta prolongada de autocontrol. El autor no propone abandonar las redes ("live off in the woods") sino asumir que mejorar tu calidad de vida es responsabilidad tuya, no de una megacorporación.
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-017-070
- **tipo:** término-acuñado
- **titulo:** The "digital dark ages": an era of distraction, close-mindedness and cheap dopamine, analogous to the Middle Ages before the Renaissance.
- **desarrollo:** The Middle Ages ("Dark Ages") were a time of war, famine and pandemics; what came after — the Renaissance — set the scene for modern civilization and birthed many thinkers and artists. "History repeats itself and the digital dark ages are among us, but there is a light on the horizon." The digital dark ages are "a time of distraction, close-mindedness and cheap dopamine." We've lost touch with, and lost trust in, the belief systems that used to give individuals certainty and a positive outlook. If we don't take it on ourselves to create our own sense of purpose and order our lives in alignment with it, it will be hard to maintain "the little sanity that we have left." The implied "light on the horizon" is a new renaissance (stated metaphorically, not elaborated).
- **ejemplos:** Middle Ages → Renaissance.
- **cita:** "the digital dark ages are a time of distraction close-mindedness and cheap dopamine"
- **terminos:** digital dark ages, cheap dopamine
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental

# SECCIÓN 1.6 — Vivir en piloto automático


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-12: Autopilot living (to autocomplete living)
- idea: La mayoría está "asleep": vive en piloto automático hacia metas que la sociedad le asignó de niño. Una meta necesita un sistema, y el sistema mejora por ensayo y error. El diagnóstico práctico consiste en preguntarse "why am I doing this?": si no hay respuesta o no te gusta, estás en piloto automático o en survival mode. Como ejercicio, el autor propone auditar tu vida con un cuaderno, anotar todo lo que haces y por qué, y tratar como distracción lo que no tenga buena respuesta, porque es energía gastada en los sueños de otros. Proyección a futuro: con la IA, mucha gente puede pasar de "autopilot living" a "autocomplete living", es decir, de que le asignen el trabajo a que se lo hagan, salvo quienes valoren su agencia.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: extensión en 2025: el piloto automático se proyecta a la era de la IA como "autocomplete living" (U-012-152). Es una ampliación, no un cambio.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

The author's gloss: people are living on autopilot; goals were assigned to them by society when they were children. Logical chain introduced here: goals require a system to be achieved, and a system takes trial and error to become more efficient.

## CLÚSTER C-T01a-28: Leave the drama of society (or you're an NPC)
- idea: Hay que dejar el "drama of society" (elecciones, peleas de celebridades "fabricated to make them money and keep you dumb"): todo ciclo que te emociona inconscientemente "narrows your mind and makes you dumb" y te hace adoptar metas y problemas ajenos que encuadran tu atención y percepción, hasta que olvidas tus propias metas. El cambio llega cambiándote a ti mismo y contagiando tu obsesión al perseguir el trabajo de tu vida (el dueño de una cervecería que reparte cerveza con pasión), no imponiendo creencias. Ante el drama de la semana, la única respuesta debería ser "I don't care. Otherwise you're an NPC".
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna; ver E-10 (tensión con "elevar la consciencia colectiva" y con la empatía de C-T01a-29).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-023-182
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Leave society's drama: emotional cycles narrow your mind and make you adopt others' goals; change happens by changing yourself and passing on your obsession
- **desarrollo:** "Leave the drama of society": the starving kids in Africa, the next election, celebrity beef "fabricated to make them money and keep you dumb" — what will actually solve the problem? Election cycles or any cycle that raises emotions unconsciously "narrows your mind and makes you dumb", making you adopt goals and problems others have for you, which frame attention and perception so you can't notice information for your goals "because you forgot your goals". Is crying and changing your profile picture going to solve it, or upskilling, pursuing your goals, doing your life's work and "creating change on the individual level that ripples out"? "You make a change by changing yourself", and pursuing your life's work "you pass along your obsession to others". Projecting and forcing beliefs on others only creates more destruction; they won't accept it. "When people ask what your thoughts are on insert the flavor of the week drama, your only response should be: I don't care. Otherwise you're an NPC".
- **ejemplos:** A beer company owner who hands out beer with passion and effort: customers are happy, make it part of their routine, pass the joy on.
- **cita:** "you make a change by changing yourself"; "your only response should be I don't care otherwise you're an NPC"
- **terminos:** drama of society; pass along your obsession; NPC
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-181
- **fuente:** Life Is A Video Game (Here's How You Win).md, 40:02-41:27, 2024-07-21
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T01b-05: We are a robotic species (cycles)
- idea: "We are such a robotic species, we have much less self-control than we believe": la vida personal corre en ciclos (ingesta, excreción, sexo, trabajo) que no elegimos conscientemente; hay ciclos propios de metas (culturista: corte/volumen, 5–6 comidas) y de negocio ("high highs imply low lows", se acepta el ciclo). Marco anidado de arriba hacia abajo: cósmico → mundial → cultural → societal → social → personal (espejeo del lenguaje corporal, Halloween/Navidad/Acción de Gracias, ropa de temporada). No les damos pensamiento consciente porque son cíclicos y condicionados; volverlos conscientes es parte de salir de la Matrix.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

- **desarrollo:** "We are such a robotic species, we have much less self-control than we believe we do". As evidence he lists cycles in personal life: an ingestion cycle (what we eat), an excretion cycle (going to the bathroom daily, without thinking about it), a sex cycle ("even if you don't have a partner"), a work cycle. These micro cycles run day to day without conscious choice. Cycles also vary with hobbies and goals.

- **desarrollo:** Personal cycles vary with hobbies or goals. A bodybuilder has cutting and bulking cycles and a more frequent ingestion cycle (five to six meals a day) that "kind of controls their life". There are also business cycles, because business is fairly unpredictable: some content does very well, some very badly; some months are high, some low. He can control that to an extent (income, content), but "overall the high highs aren't sustainable, high highs imply that there are going to be low lows". Acceptance of the cycle is part of the stance.

### U-023-019
- **tipo:** framework
- **titulo:** Nested cycles from the top down: cosmic, worldly, cultural, societal, social, personal
- **desarrollo:** The world and the cosmos as a whole "seem to occur in cycles from the top down", and these impact human behavior. The order he gives: Cosmic → worldly → cultural → societal → social → personal ("there may be more in between that but those are major things"). Examples at the different levels: social — mirroring someone's body language as a conversation goes on, a cycle you may not be conscious of or control; cultural — in the States celebrating Halloween, Christmas, Thanksgiving, for which we prepare and act unconsciously; worldly/seasonal — changing clothes when winter or summer comes. We don't give these conscious thought because they are cyclical, repetitive and conditioned into our brains.
- **ejemplos:** Mirroring in conversation; Halloween, Christmas, Thanksgiving; seasonal clothing changes.
- **cita:** "it goes like Cosmic worldly cultural societal social personal"
- **terminos:** cycles from the top down
