# SECCIÓN 1.7 — La mente cerrada: la ideología como identidad


## CLÚSTER C-T01a-25: Deliberate ignorance (closed minds that resist your path)
- idea: Quienes tienen la vida que tú quieres son "less than 1%". El otro 99% opina sobre cómo vivir después de haber desperdiciado la suya: te pide realismo, presupuesto y diversión en tus 20, y un empleo que odias hasta los 50, todo desde una "industrial mindset". En el fondo "they just don't want to feel inferior to you". La respuesta es la "deliberate ignorance": puedes considerar la idea, pero no dejarla entrar en tu mente. Busca al <1%, experimenta con su consejo sin tomarlo como ley y hazlo tuyo por ensayo y error. La resistencia se explica porque las creencias cristalizan alrededor de los 25 años y se vuelven dogma (y las generaciones mayores moldean la cultura), y porque los padres que dejaron de aprender tienen "minds harden up": "people hate what they don't understand". Las mentes cerradas proyectan opiniones y declaran imposible lo que quieres. Heurística: "if a boomer tells you to get a real job you're on the right track", porque "every great idea started as blasphemy".
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna dentro del clúster; ver E-10 por la tensión con la apertura mental (C-T01a-29).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-025-173
- **tipo:** advertencia
- **titulo:** Trap 1: Don't listen to anyone who doesn't have the life you want — they are 99%, and they push you toward realism, budgets, partying and a job you hate until 50.
- **desarrollo:** People with the life you want are extremely rare, "less than 1% of the population"; the other 99% "blabber about how you should live your life when they wasted their own." They insist the greatest things to do are what they did. Bring them near-impossible goals: they'll say be more realistic, create a budget, invest your measly savings, go have fun and make the most of your 20s (without knowing what that means), and set yourself up in a boring mechanical life through a job you hate until 50, "as if that's some like twisted rite of passage." They have an "industrial mindset" (school, job, because your ancestors did it) and don't understand how technology is changing career paths. Your alternatives don't register — it puts them in survival mode because they fear you won't survive. "Many people say they want the best for you, but in reality, they just don't want to feel inferior to you."
- **ejemplos:** advice to be realistic, budget, invest savings

## CLÚSTER C-T01a-29: Close-mindedness (the greatest trap of the 21st century)
- idea: La close-mindedness es "your inability to see beyond your narrow perspective in any situation", la trampa más grande del siglo XXI y otra forma de la "quick fix mindset": busca demostrar que los demás están equivocados en lugar de ponerse a prueba a sí misma, y no busca terreno común (ejemplo: las guerras en los comentarios de Reddit o Instagram). Toma "the smallest story possible" e ignora el arco de la "one song" (uni-verse): en un punto bajo cree que todo es así. Tiene tres señales: (1) suposiciones y expectativas negativas, porque el condicionamiento fija lo que crees posible ("your identity houses your perspective and your perspective houses your beliefs"); (2) una perspectiva demasiado egoísta, en la que cada uno defiende su autoimagen (partidos políticos, modelos de negocio; el autor admite caer en esto él mismo); (3) perder el punto por interpretar literalmente o sacar un verso de la canción. El método es transferir la consciencia al otro, suponer una meta positiva detrás de sus actos, estudiar la perspectiva opuesta y ponerse a prueba ("everyone is right and everyone is wrong"). Ejemplo: leer The Power of Now siendo ateo y superar la incomodidad con la palabra "God". Abrirse lleva tiempo ("that's why it's called development").
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: ninguna dentro del clúster; ver E-10 (tensión entre la empatía y el relativismo de 2023 y los descartes tajantes posteriores).
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-017-073
- **tipo:** definición
- **titulo:** Close-mindedness — the greatest trap of the 21st century — is the inability to see beyond your narrow perspective in any situation.
- **desarrollo:** The three solaces (U-017-072) are grouped under "the greatest trap of the 21st century: close-mindedness." "The most dangerous habit young people can form is close-mindedness; you are capable of more than you've been told you are." Definition: "your inability to see beyond your narrow perspective in any situation," and worse if that perspective only shines a negative light. Close-mindedness seeks to prove others wrong rather than prove yourself wrong and doesn't seek common ground. It is "another embodiment of the quick fix mindset," which is why it encapsulates distractions and momentary pleasures.
- **ejemplos:** See U-017-074.
- **cita:** "the most dangerous habit young people can form is closed mindedness"
- **terminos:** close-mindedness, greatest trap of the 21st century, quick fix mindset
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-072
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 2:37–4:17, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-074
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Social media comment sections show close-mindedness: miss the point, cherry-pick, spin negatively, prove others wrong for a quick hit.
- **desarrollo:** On Reddit or Instagram, it's common for someone to completely miss the point, misinterpret, cherry-pick something, spin it negatively, and leave a comment that creates negativity and turns people away from the true message. E.g., someone reads a polarizing political post, gets offended, and comments in a way that conveniently leaves out the perspective that would prove themselves wrong; perspectives clash; no common ground. It's a quick fix: you get "that quick hit of whether it's dopamine or just a bad feeling that you are addicted to" — "that's why you're making the post, why else would you make it?" Zoom out: your post does nothing in the grand scheme; you're wasting time, and time is important.
- **ejemplos:** Political post comment wars on Reddit/Instagram.
- **cita:** "you seek to prove others wrong rather than proving yourself wrong"
- **terminos:** quick fix
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-073
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 2:37–4:17, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-075
- **tipo:** metáfora
- **titulo:** The universe as "one song": life has a story structure, and close-mindedness takes the smallest story (one low point) and ignores the arc.
- **desarrollo:** "The universe is an infinite song: uni-verse, one song." Songs are symbolic of stories with story-like structure, reflected also in games — the highs, lows, conflict, rising action, climax, falling action, happy ending. We find interest and meaning in that; it's why people get obsessed with video games, why those who see business as a game get obsessed with business, and why those who can zoom out and create a game from a situation find enjoyment in most of life. Close-mindedness fails to see the overarching story; it takes "the smallest story possible," like a current conflict, ignores the universal perspective, and expects it to make sense on its own. If you're at a low point and your mind sees only that, not understanding that after a low comes a high (and before a low, a high), with a climax eventually, you get trapped in a negative thought loop.
- **ejemplos:** Video games; business as a game; life's low points.
- **cita:** "the universe is an infinite song UNI verse Universe one song"
- **terminos:** one song, overarching story, smallest story possible, negative thought loop
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-073
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 4:17–5:55, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-077
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Sign 1 of close-mindedness: negative assumption and expectation — your conditioning sets limits on what you think is possible.
- **desarrollo:** First sign: assumption and expectation, "very much so in the negative sense." A vision is a kind of positive assumption/expectation; negative ones can be extremely dangerous. "Your reality is determined by your conditioning, that is, your identity houses your perspective and your perspective houses your beliefs, thoughts and prior experiences" that you hold as truth; for most, this worldview is shared with parents, teachers and others. If all you know is school → work → retirement, behavior follows because conditioning limits what you think possible; you won't act to actualize a possibility you don't know. These are expectations you'll work in alignment with. Even a vision you think is positive, if you haven't zoomed out to see the consequences of that path, is "a masquerade": negative and not conducive to what you want. The web of expectations eventually leads to behavior that actualizes them.
- **ejemplos:** School-work-retirement.
- **cita:** "your identity houses your perspective and your perspective houses your beliefs thoughts and prior experiences"
- **terminos:** conditioning, negative assumption and expectation, masquerade, web of expectations
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-076
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 6:41–8:19, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-078
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Sign 2 of close-mindedness: an overly selfish perspective — everyone acts toward a goal, usually to survive their identity.
- **desarrollo:** Everyone acting is acting toward a goal (conscious, subconscious or unconscious), mostly aligned with survival. Since humans survive on a conceptual level, they work to survive their identity or maintain their narrow perspective, acting to preserve their self-image. Examples: die-hard Republicans or Democrats arguing online, some willing to die over a split black-and-white party rather than pulling truth from both for a holistic perspective. Same in business: people arguing which business model is best; freelancers say freelancing is best because that's what they've done; same for agency owners, e-commerce, startups, software. He admits being a victim of this himself, though he tries to make clear the one-person business model is for someone with a specific set of goals.
- **ejemplos:** Republicans vs. Democrats; freelancers vs. agency owners vs. e-commerce.
- **cita:** "they're going to act in a way that preserves their self-image"
- **terminos:** selfish perspective, survive on a conceptual level, self-image
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-009, U-017-076
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 8:19–9:58, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** He admits he does this too with the one-person business model.


### U-017-079
- **tipo:** método
- **titulo:** Overcome selfish perspective: transfer consciousness into the other, assume a positive goal behind their actions, and try to prove yourself wrong.
- **desarrollo:** For a more holistic version of truth: (1) "practice transferring consciousness into that person" — have empathy, or at least piece together a positive assumption about the goal they're acting toward; (2) be willing to prove yourself wrong: if you push ideas as law, study the opposite perspective and empathize with someone pushing the opposite. Then you hold both versions of truth and "unlock this wisdom or intelligence that allows you to navigate between those two domains." Collecting more perspectives aligned with others' goals gives you "so much more firepower" to make your own thing work holistically. Also: some Western beliefs would get you executed in other countries — they don't care how right you think you are. From the universe's perspective (division, unity and self-reflection), "everyone is right and everyone is wrong, it's all relative; this reality is subjective."
- **ejemplos:** Beliefs punishable by execution in other countries.
- **cita:** "everyone is right and everyone is wrong it's all relative this reality is subjective"
- **terminos:** transferring consciousness, holistic version of truth, both versions of truth
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-078
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 9:58–11:33, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-080
- **tipo:** concepto
- **titulo:** Sign 3 of close-mindedness: missing the point, often from literal interpretation of words or taking a lyric out of the song.
- **desarrollo:** Missing the point often comes from literal interpretation. If, as a non-Christian, he reads the Bible literally (word-for-word "this is what happened"), it'll turn him off; read metaphorically, as symbolic of some truth, he can learn something. It also relates to taking one piece of the story/game/universe ("one song"): you wouldn't take one lyric out of a song, or one sentence he says, out of context and expect it to make sense alone. You must understand the goal people are working toward; e.g., this video's goal is to open your mind, so taking something and closing your mind misses the point.
- **ejemplos:** Reading the Bible literally vs. metaphorically; lyric out of a song.
- **cita:** "you wouldn't take one lyric from a song ... take it out of context ... and expect it to make it Sense on its own"
- **terminos:** missing the point, literal interpretation, metaphorical perspective
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-075
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 11:33–13:06, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-081
- **tipo:** historia
- **titulo:** Reading Eckhart Tolle's "The Power of Now" as an atheist: working past the discomfort with the word "God."
- **desarrollo:** When he first read The Power of Now he identified as an atheist; whenever the book mentioned the concept of God, he got "this weird feeling." He tried hard to overcome it because he knew something in the book could help him, tried to see past it, and over time dissolved or worked past that problem. Illustrates overcoming literal interpretation / missing the point.
- **ejemplos:** The Power of Now.
- **cita:** "whenever he would mention the concept of God per se then I would get this weird feeling in me but I really tried to overcome that"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** de-tercero:Eckhart Tolle (book referenced)
- **nivel:** complementario
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-080
- **fuente:** Distraction Is The Greatest Trap Of The 21st Century (How To Avoid).md, 11:33–12:21, 2023-01-22
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T01b-29: Closed-mindedness (the mental home, ideology as law)
- idea: Una ideología (política o de salud) es un "mental home" (cita de McKenna): si alguien amenaza sus cimientos, sientes una amenaza, una reacción de estrés y emociones fuera de control, y no disfrutas la vida porque no entiendes el impacto de la mente cerrada (ciclos políticos). La vida es "a conglomeration of perspectives"; aceptar una ideología como ley cierra la mente a toda oportunidad (él cayó en la trampa del "Raw Liver"). "Everything is a half truth": creer que todo exitoso online es secretamente infeliz es "the most closed-minded perspective you could ever adopt"; cuestiona tus creencias limitantes, que nunca tendrás acceso al estado mental del otro. Lo mismo vale para su propio método: "principles are few, the man who grasps principles can successfully select his own method"; no lo tomes como ley: cuestiónalo, pruébalo, y si encuentras algo mejor, crea contenido sobre eso.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: ninguna
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-023-020
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Political cycles: when someone threatens the mental home of your ideology you have an unconscious stress response
- **desarrollo:** People should become conscious of political cycles, during which many get overly emotional "simply because they're unconscious" and have expectations and beliefs about the mental constructions others have created for certain individuals. Linking to the McKenna quote: if you subscribe to a political ideology, it is a "mental home" you live in. If someone threatens that house, tries to tear its foundation apart, you as a being feel threatened, have a stress response, get emotional without your own control, are not mindful of it, "and then things are going to go to [ __ ]". You won't enjoy your life because you don't understand the impacts of closed-mindedness.
- **ejemplos:** Upcoming political cycles (video from November 2022).
- **cita:** "if you subscribe to a political ideology and someone threatens that house of a mental home that you live in... you as a being are going to feel threatened"
- **terminos:** mental home; mental construction
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-023-011, U-023-014
- **fuente:** The Matrix Is Real (How To Break Free With Your Mind).md, 13:04-13:56, 2022-11-20
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-007-008
- **tipo:** advertencia
- **titulo:** Accepting any ideology as law closes your mind; life is a conglomeration of perspectives
- **desarrollo:** Dan warns about adopting belief systems wholesale, in health or anything else. Life is "a conglomeration of perspectives and goals that people are trying to hit, problems and benefits" that together form a singular perspective that makes someone believe something. You must see multiple perspectives. Accepting a specific ideology as law is closing your mind to any opportunity you can have.
- **ejemplos:** Someone says "Raw Liver is the best thing you can eat"; Dan says he got stuck in that trap.
- **cita:** "accepting a specific ideology as law is just closing your mind off to any opportunity you can have"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-007-005
- **fuente:** If You Are High Value, Start A One-Person Business.md, 3:27-4:18, 2022-10-02
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-007-069
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** "All online successful people are secretly unhappy" is a half-truth and a closed-minded limiting belief
- **desarrollo:** Objection: some social media people live flashy lifestyles, are too extreme, or claim to be happy all the time, so people conclude all rich people and influencers are unhappy deep down, know nothing, wear a facade. Dan: everything is a half-truth; some are that way. But believing every person online is that way and their success is "a fart in the wind" is "the most closed-minded perspective you could ever adopt". Why aren't you questioning that? That's curiosity. Question your own limiting beliefs and reactions: what if some are extremely happy? You'd have to be in their exact shoes, with access to their state of mind (which you'll never have), to know for sure. Therefore it isn't worth worrying about; it's worth working toward if you want it, rather than talking badly about it and throwing others off a good path. You'd have to experience the lifestyle to know. You can be happy in any phase of life; that's what spirituality is about: whether you play "the external game of riches" or not, you can be happy and enlightened.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "everything is a half truth"
- **terminos:** half truth; limiting beliefs; external game of riches; curiosity
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** none
- **fuente:** 11 Lessons From Growing A 7-Figure One Person Business.md, 1:37-3:17, 2022-11-06
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-007-158
- **tipo:** fuente-de-tercero
- **titulo:** "As to methods there may be a million... but principles are few": don't take Dan's method as law
- **desarrollo:** Dan quotes (without naming the author): "as to methods there may be a million and then some but principles are few the man who grasps principles can successfully select his own method". He says this whole letter was partly principles but mostly his method. So don't take it as law: question it, test it, experience it, and if you find a better solution, create content about it. This isn't the only way of doing business.
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "principles are few the man who grasp principles can successfully select his own method"
- **terminos:** principles; methods

- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** none
- **fuente:** The One Person Business Roadmap (99% Make This Mistake).md, 38:53-39:44, 2022-12-18
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T02a-09: Self-limiting identity labels and ideological attachment
- idea: Cuando una creencia, ideología o etiqueta ("I am X") se vuelve parte de la identidad, cuestionarla dispara lucha o huida "como si la vida dependiera de ello", aunque sea "just a thought in your head". El que se identifica con la dieta keto o con una religión deja de experimentar y de extraer principios propios. Devon Eriksen lleva la idea más lejos: cualquier etiqueta (anarcocapitalista, artista, incluso "soy hombre") puede volverse autolimitante e impedir examinarla como herramienta. Es uno de los mayores riesgos para la agencia, porque probar cosas nuevas amenaza la fuente del propio valor. Lo humano es la "executive function" (decidir qué quiero, con qué herramientas y qué herramienta nueva crear) y ampliar la caja de herramientas. Las identidades colectivas (equipo, empresa, familia) forman un ego colectivo: no son malas, pero hay que verlas y ser "like water". En el extremo está la "Christ Consciousness": una identidad no atada a nada, que permite ver las situaciones como son.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: convive con C-T02a-11 (identificarse a propósito con una identidad positiva). Ver "Evolución y tensiones", caso 1.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-014-007
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Humans react to challenged beliefs they're attached to as if their survival were threatened
- **desarrollo:** Pertaining to survival and human development, the author claims humans are "really the only being" that react to having beliefs they're attached to challenged in a way similar to how they reacted in "past lives" when survival was threatened. Someone very set on, e.g., a religious belief will, when it's challenged, do anything to confirm or defend it "like their life depends on it": they go into fight or flight, focus and perspective narrow onto that one thing, they attach to it and nothing else matters. "It's just a belief, it's just a thought in your head." This causes pain and suffering and can bring past traumatic experiences into your present sensory experience.
- **ejemplos:** Someone very set on a religious belief having it challenged.
- **cita:** "it's just a belief it's just a thought in your head and that's what you're narrowing in on"
- **terminos:** attached, fight or flight, narrowed focus
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-014-005
- **fuente:** Stop Trying To Be Unique... Do This Instead.md, 3:58-5:00, 2022-05-28
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-021-010
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Religion and keto diet: when an identity-attached belief is questioned, people react as if physically threatened
- **desarrollo:** To illustrate conceptual survival: if you subscribe to a religion and someone says another religion is better or questions your beliefs, how do you feel? How tied are you to "something so static that you can't break free of"? If someone threatens it, do you feel threatened physically, have the stress response, get angry and reactive? The same applies to a diet model: many fitness people love the keto diet and adopt it "as a part of who they are" because it got them results after a bad diet — without "experimenting with everything and noting the principles or truths between them all and then creating their own path." When someone says "I love to eat carbs," they feel threatened and react ("you're gonna die because you're eating so many carbs"). The author says this "stems from a lack of understanding and awareness." The contrast implied: experimenting with everything and extracting common principles to create your own path instead of identifying with one model.
- **ejemplos:** Religion being questioned; keto dieter reacting to someone who loves carbs.
- **cita:** "they identify with the keto diet and then ... someone comes in and says like oh I love to eat carbs how do they feel"
- **terminos:** ninguno
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-021-009
- **fuente:** How To Reprogram Your Mind & Build Your Intelligence.md, 7:17–8:01, 2023-09-24
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-006-029
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Adopting a philosophical label as identity makes you unable to critically examine it as a tool.
- **desarrollo:** Asked by Dan Koe whether he has a guiding religion or philosophy with a label, Devon Eriksen answers "you have to beware of self-limiting beliefs." He would describe his views as somewhat anarcho-capitalistic and can call himself an anarcho-capitalist "sometimes for convenience" ("we don't want to fight the great linguistic Crusade"), "but if I do it so consistently within my own head then I have made that my identity and then I am unable to critically examine anarcho capitalism and to critique it as a tool and say does this or doesn't this serve my goals right now."
- **ejemplos:** Anarcho-capitalism as a convenient label.
- **cita:** "then I have made that my identity and then I am unable to critically examine anarcho capitalism"
- **terminos:** self-limiting beliefs; identity; tool
- **origen:** de-tercero:Devon Eriksen
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-006-028
- **fuente:** The Most Important Skill To Learn In The Next 10 Years With Devon Eriksen.md, 38:38–39:49, 2024-12-08
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-006-032
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Every "I am X" self-description can become self-limiting, even a biological fact like "I am male."
- **desarrollo:** Devon Eriksen: "anytime you say to yourself I am an artist I am an author I am an anarcho capitalist I am a republican I am a Democrat I am a communist... I am white I am male... all of these things are useful ways to describe yourself but you have to be careful with them because any of these can become self-limiting." Even "I am male," which "is a biological fact": writing the sequel to Theft of Fire, Miranda is a point-of-view character for part of the book, so he has to write from a woman's perspective. "The fact that I am male is the truth but I have to set that tool down and pick up another tool of oh this is my mental model of how women work and how what it feels like to be one in order to achieve the task."
- **ejemplos:** Writing Miranda's point of view in the sequel to Theft of Fire.
- **cita:** "I have to set that tool down and pick up another tool"
- **terminos:** self-limiting; tool; mental model
- **origen:** de-tercero:Devon Eriksen
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-006-031
- **fuente:** The Most Important Skill To Learn In The Next 10 Years With Devon Eriksen.md, 43:36–44:46, 2024-12-08
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-006-033
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** Self-limiting identity beliefs are one of the biggest risks to agency because new attempts threaten the identity from which you derive your sense of value.
- **desarrollo:** Devon Eriksen: "one of the biggest reasons why people are unwilling to take risks and try new things one of the biggest things that can be a real risk to someone's agency are these self-limiting beliefs I can't do that I can't try that because it threatens my sense of identity my sense of Who I Am from where I get my sense of value." If you say "I am an artist and I am worthwhile because I am a great artist," you're threatened not only by AI that can draw but "also threatened by doing things that aren't art." "Ultimately we are not a tool for doing any particular task we are here to navigate our own lives in a way that makes sense to us." "What it really means to develop as a human being is to have this toolbox have the ability to expand the toolbox and be able to express whatever part of our capacity helps us to do what we want."
- **ejemplos:** The artist who ties self-worth to being a great artist.
- **cita:** "what it really means to develop as a human being is to have this toolbox have the ability to expand the toolbox"
- **terminos:** self-limiting beliefs; sense of identity; toolbox
- **origen:** de-tercero:Devon Eriksen
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-006-028; U-006-023
- **fuente:** The Most Important Skill To Learn In The Next 10 Years With Devon Eriksen.md, 44:46–46:09, 2024-12-08
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-006-037
- **tipo:** historia
- **titulo:** Devon Eriksen's move from software engineering to science fiction: discovering his real goal was creativity, not "being a software engineer."
- **desarrollo:** Devon spent many years as a software engineer and was told he was very good. He always had friction ("attrition" in the transcript) with the people he worked for, whom he experienced as very shortsighted: he wanted to build for the next 5 or 10 years; they'd say "here's a list of features that we need to get done by our next two-month release cycle and why are you going off the rails building all this foundational cryptography toolkits... just write the login page." He realized "these people didn't trust me because I did what was good for them rather than what they said to do," and also that he was giving vent to a creative impulse. Had he said "my life goal is to be a really great software engineer," he would have been stuck in a self-limiting identity belief. It took time ("I got gray hair") to figure out he did it "because software is the ultimate set of Lego blocks" and he wants to create, to "bring new stuff out of my imagination and into the world," which employers didn't want. "My real goal is I want to imagine something that no one has imagined before and make it real." So he retired early and started writing science fiction, which "blossomed out" into political opinion writing and philosophical musings: "I take something out of my brain and I show it to people and they go ooh and that is what I really wanted out of being a software engineer all along."
- **ejemplos:** Two-month release cycle vs. foundational cryptography toolkits; "just write the login page."
- **cita:** "software is the ultimate set of Lego blocks"
- **terminos:** creative impulse; self-limiting identity belief
- **origen:** de-tercero:Devon Eriksen
- **nivel:** complementario
- **prerrequisitos:** U-006-036
- **fuente:** The Most Important Skill To Learn In The Next 10 Years With Devon Eriksen.md, 51:22–54:55, 2024-12-08
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-006-027
- **tipo:** principio
- **titulo:** What a human is for is executive function: deciding what you want, how to get it with the tools you have, and what new tool to create if they're insufficient.
- **desarrollo:** Devon Eriksen: "what a human being is really for is that executive function is deciding what do I want what are my goals what do I wish to achieve okay how can I do that with the tools I have and if I can't do that with the tools I have what kind of new tool do I need to create." Sequence: (1) want/goals; (2) means with existing tools; (3) creation of new tools. "When you are able to navigate your life on this level then chat GPT doesn't feel like a threat to you."
- **ejemplos:** ninguno
- **cita:** "what kind of new tool do I need to create"
- **terminos:** executive function
- **origen:** de-tercero:Devon Eriksen
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-006-022
- **fuente:** The Most Important Skill To Learn In The Next 10 Years With Devon Eriksen.md, 36:57–37:15, 2024-12-08
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-017-088
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Collective identities (sports team, corporate job, family) form a collective ego; be aware, and identify in mind/spirit, not physicality.
- **desarrollo:** Non-religious examples: on a sports team (e.g., "the Snowbirds"), identifying as a Snowbird forms a collective ego the team works to survive; that spirit spreads to fans and affects how the game goes when the team acts in that energy. Same with a corporate job (perspective limited to that job) or a family. These aren't bad; become aware of them; be like water able to pour into different cups. Clarification: identifying "in mind or spirit here, not physicality or intellect" — if you want to identify as something "hyper limiting" like a toaster oven or an Apache attack helicopter (memes), "be my guest," or revisit the part about intentionally missing the point — unless the point is humor, which is useful and harmless unless people intentionally miss the point.
- **ejemplos:** Snowbirds team; corporate job; family; toaster/Apache helicopter memes.
- **cita:** "the spirit of that team will form a collective ego that the the team Works to survive"
- **terminos:** collective ego, spirit
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-017-086, U-017-083

Koe disclaims lecturing on religion, ideology or dogma ("the exact opposite of what I'm pointing at"). He points at "first order being not second order knowing": being is the landscape of everything; knowing emerges from it; you can't fold knowing under being—"it is impossible to know because it can't be put into words because it came from words or words came from it" (as transcribed; ambiguous).

## CLÚSTER C-T02b-03: Ideology as identity (keep your identity small; philosophies as tools)
- idea: Cuando una ideología (política, dieta, religión, modelo de negocio) se vuelve identidad, cualquier desacuerdo se vive como amenaza, y así es imposible llegar a la "holistic truth". Según Dan, lo que hace que una persona deje de pensar es fusionar creencias con quien es: el world model fija la forma del pensamiento, así que conviene adoptar el más abarcador posible. El matiz es que esto no obliga a rechazar a ambos bandos; solo implica no sentirse amenazado. Los antídotos que propone son tres: mantener la identidad pequeña (Paul Graham, y el consistency bias de Cialdini, citados por Dickie Bush), entender las filosofías como herramientas y no como metas (Devon Eriksen) y buscar activamente demostrarse equivocado. El límite: no se puede ayudar a quien no quiere ser ayudado (The Kybalion).
- nivel: fundamental
- evolución: choca con identificarse a propósito con un label o con el higher self (ver Evolución: "intensidad de la identificación")
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-016-146
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** When an ideology becomes identity, any threat to it feels like a threat to survival, which makes discovering the holistic truth impossible
- **desarrollo:** "This is similar to how Republicans and Democrats fight or how carnivores and vegans fight: people get trapped in a specific ideology and they identify with it, so it becomes their identity and something that threatens their survival when the idea of their identity is threatened." In "narrow-minded discussions" such as "the online business person versus the everything-is-a-scam person or the everyone-should-go-out-and-live-in-the-woods-and-meditate-all-day person," "there is no chance at discovering the holistic truth of the situation." "Obviously both extremes are wrong," so Dan sets out to "deconstruct what is at least a bit closer to truth."
- **ejemplos:** Republicans vs Democrats; carnivores vs vegans; online business person vs "everything is a scam" person vs "live in the woods and meditate" person.
- **cita:** "people get trapped in a specific ideology and they identify with it so it becomes their identity"
- **terminos:** holistic truth; ideology; identity
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-016-145
- **fuente:** People Dumber Than You Are Making Millions.md, 0:00-0:51, 2023-11-12
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-022-100
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** What makes people stop thinking is identity: fusing beliefs into who you are; your world model sets the shape of your thinking.
- **desarrollo:** "Stupid thinking is just when you stop thinking. But what makes a person stop thinking?… The answer here is identity and you latching on to beliefs, ideas, or constructs and just making them a part of who you are because that's inherently limiting, especially if those ideas… are small." "Your ability to think is determined by your world model… by your beliefs. So you should, in my opinion, adopt the most encompassing world model that you can." If you willingly adopt something dogmatic, ideological, one-dimensional, "your thinking is going to take the shape of that." Die-hard Republicans or Democrats can't think out of the bubble, "will never be able to find the truth… just arguing in circles." Nuance: this doesn't mean you can't adopt values of either side or think sides hold certain truths, nor that you must turn against both. "It simply means that you don't need to feel threatened when someone else disagrees with you, because that threat is signaling that you think… that's who you are." Applies to every domain — e.g., people arguing endlessly online about Alex Hormozi's methods; religion; vocation.
- **ejemplos:** Die-hard partisans; Hormozi-method arguments.
- **cita:** "That threat is signaling that you think… that's who you are."
- **terminos:** identity; world model; most encompassing world model
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** fundamental
- **prerrequisitos:** U-022-071
- **fuente:** How To Think Like A Strategic Genius (5-Dimensional Thinking).md [29:39]-[31:40] (2026-02-22)

The more labels, the more you "fall victim to consistency bias which is [Cialdini]'s like number one driver of human behavior is aligning on some kind of identity you formed for yourself." Dickie's interpretation: the source of suffering Dan described "is literally acting out of alignment to some kind of label or identity that you've crafted for yourself."

### U-006-030
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** The seed-oil ban example: identifying with a political philosophy makes you serve the philosophy instead of your goals.
- **desarrollo:** Devon Eriksen describes himself often as an anarcho-capitalist or libertarian and gets other libertarians "doing the favorite hobby of Libertarians which is calling other Libertarians not real Libertarians." He says "I would like to see seed oils banned" and they reply "that is a statist position." His diagnosis: "you have identified with a political philosophy to the point where you are thinking about how to serve the political philosophy not how to achieve your goals the political philosophy was made to serve you not the other way around." He calls himself an anarchist/libertarian because he wants people to be free, to have control over their own destinies; with that motivating goal clear he can ask, in a specific situation, what move gives people more control over their destinies: "which is more important the freedom to poison people or the freedom to not be poisoned." Dan Koe draws the nuance: "the thing that makes a person most free isn't always absolute freedom"; if someone doesn't have the choice or doesn't know, that isn't inherently freeing.
- **ejemplos:** Seed oils ban; "freedom to poison people or the freedom to not be poisoned."
- **cita:** "the political philosophy was made to serve you not the other way around"

## CLÚSTER C-T16a-23: Diet ideology vs clarity not chaos
- idea: El ejemplo del converso vegano: alguien con poca energía busca una solución, adopta una dieta como ley, ve resultados y se vuelve su "profeta", defendiéndola como si su identidad dependiera de ello. Pero lo que funcionó es independiente de la ideología: más comida nutritiva, acciones al servicio de la nueva identidad y "Clarity not chaos" (menos opciones; "you add rules to a game... and games are fun to play"); gran parte de la energía es psychic energy liberada. Las dietas de moda son "finite games": la energía viene de la claridad de la rutina prestada y se desvanece, por eso se salta de una a otra; hay que estudiar principios generales y armar el propio enfoque, usando la dieta como lente que se pone y se quita.
- nivel: intermedio
- evolución: el mismo ejemplo se reinterpreta en tres marcos sucesivos; ver "Evolución y tensiones".
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-026-255
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** The vegan convert: results attributed to the ideology came from nutrient-dense food, identity-driven actions, and the clarity of a regimen
- **desarrollo:** Imagine you're in a season of struggle with low energy, a problem you want to solve, so "you're subconsciously in search for a solution". Someone pops onto your timeline "with confidence and conviction saying something like the vegan diet is going to solve all of these specific problems", "because that's what marketers do and there's not a problem with this", but you must think critically and holistically, "and not just latch on to this diet ideology". Common path (he has done similar, "not specifically with veganism"): "from this unenlightened state you clean up your diet you follow their advice as if it were law and you see the results", then you "attach to that diet ideology and become a prophet of it", attribute results to that method, and "defend that ideology or that identity ... like their life depended on it ... since their identity is threatened". Reality: "what happened is independent of veganism": you ate more nutrient-dense foods; "your actions work to survive your vegan identity"; "you had Clarity not chaos by following a disciplined nutrition regimen". "Yeah if you clean up your diet you're going to have a bit more energy but most of it is psychic energy being freed up so you actually have the space and open focus and you're not so narrowed in and stressed on solving the problem."
- **ejemplos:** Veganism.
- **cita:** "most of it is psychic energy being freed up"
- **terminos:** diet ideology; prophet; identity; psychic energy; clarity not chaos
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-026-254
- **fuente:** Happiness Is A Skill (How To Get Out Of A Rut).md, 10:18-12:48, 2023-02-26
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-020-084
- **tipo:** argumento
- **titulo:** The veganism/six-pack example: a diet works by giving clarity and rules, not because the ideology is "the" truth
- **desarrollo:** Say your goal is a six-pack and someone tells you veganism will solve all your problems. From an "unlighted state" you clean up your diet, follow it as law, see results, and attach to that ideology as the best way — because it generated clarity, which is why you felt good. What really happened (applies to any fad diet adopted as identity instead of "lens that they can take off and put on"): you ate more nutrient-dense foods; your actions worked to survive your vegan identity; and you had "Clarity not chaos" — fewer options and choices: "you add rules to a game that you were playing and games are fun to play." That is the purpose of a hierarchy of goals while staying open-minded on how to achieve them: rules or limits frame attention, eliminate distractions and make goals easier to achieve.
- **ejemplos:** Veganism for a six-pack.
- **cita:** "you add rules to a game that you were playing and games are fun to play"
- **terminos:** lens, clarity not chaos, rules to a game
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-020-083
- **fuente:** You Won’t Be The Same Person In 6 Months (Master Anything, Fast).md, 31:45–32:29, 2023-12-17
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-026-195
- **tipo:** ejemplo
- **titulo:** Fad diets are finite games: the energy comes from the clarity of an adopted routine, not the diet, so it wears off
- **desarrollo:** In health, "fad diets are an example": you have to zoom out and study the big-picture principles of diet and nutrition by studying various perspectives and coming up with your own way. Following a fad diet, you stick with the routine and feel very good for a while, "but then you're just going to fall off" and wonder why you don't feel as energetic. "Usually it's not the diet that's giving you energy it's the clarity that comes from the routine that you're adopting from someone else that is the fad diet model", and once it wears off you go pursue another one. "Carnivores are switching to rapat [?] Atkins diet doesn't even exist anymore they just all change." "You can notice these finite games and choose not to play them."
- **ejemplos:** Carnivore, Atkins.
- **cita:** "usually it's not the diet that's giving you energy it's the clarity that comes from the routine"
- **terminos:** fad diet model; finite games; big picture principles
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** U-026-192
- **fuente:** My Honest Advice For Those Who Feel Lost & Confused.md, 15:06-15:54, 2024-05-30
- **tension:** ninguna



## CLÚSTER C-T17-36: Fitness in the brand (dogmatism and Superhuman 90)
- idea: El fitness fue donde empezó, pero en 2023 dejó de hablar de él al notar que se volvía dogmático en un "pocket of advice" de Twitter sin contexto (hígado crudo, agua de manantial, "confesar" un edulcorante) porque el engagement lo reforzaba; mantenerlo exigía mucho más tiempo para educar con perspectiva holística, así que desapareció 2–3 semanas y decidió hablar solo de one-person business. En 2025 reintegró la salud con Superhuman 90, un reinicio de 90 días "no en el dominio de los negocios" (entrenamiento y nutrición con su entrenador Randy, dopamine detox, monk mode y rediseño del estilo de vida), con un mapa del día 1 al 90.
- nivel: complementario
- evolución: de abandonar el fitness como tema de marca (2023) a crear un producto de salud integrado en su marca (2025). Ver Evolución y tensiones.
- fusiones (unidades sustancialmente idénticas; usa la unión de su contenido): ninguna

### U-002-010
- **tipo:** historia
- **titulo:** Dan's off-grid realization: dropping fitness content after becoming dogmatic
- **desarrollo:** Fitness was where Dan started and experimented "to no end." He noticed himself becoming dogmatic about things promoted on Twitter, "in this little pocket of advice that didn't seem to have the right context behind it." He experimented with eating raw liver (Liver King as the extreme example), noticed some benefits, and "conformed to that mindset of like I'm doing this weird thing I'm gonna talk about it because it gets engagement it got engagement that reinforced it." He realized he was pushing advice as if it were "an absolute necessity for every single person," straying from principles and basics toward esoterica ("here's how to 10x your energy eat raw liver drink Mountain Springs water ... put salt in your water"). He felt bad when he didn't abide — "if I had an artificial sweetener I'd be like I need to tell Twitter ... I need to confess for my sins." To keep fitness in his brand he'd need to dedicate far more time to bring a holistic perspective and educate properly; that didn't feel right, so he stopped talking about it and went off grid for 2–3 weeks. He came back and sent Dickie a voice note: he would only talk about one-person business / creator economy.
- **ejemplos:** Raw liver, Liver King, mountain spring water, salt in water, confessing an artificial sweetener.
- **cita:** "I need to confess for my sins"
- **terminos:** dogmatic; pocket of advice; engagement reinforcement
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** intermedio
- **prerrequisitos:** ninguno
- **fuente:** Dan Koe & Dickie Bush On One-Person Businesses, Creative Workflows, and Lifestyle Design.md, [15:12]–[17:56], 2023-03-16
- **tension:** ninguna


### U-024-108
- **tipo:** caso
- **titulo:** Superhuman 90: a 90-day non-business reset combining training, nutrition, dopamine detox, monk mode and lifestyle redesign
- **desarrollo:** (Product with method content.) Dan Koe always had an obsession with health and fitness but never knew how to fit it into a productivity/business/philosophy brand without "go beast mode" masculine aura that wouldn't motivate people. So he built Superhuman 90, "a 90-day reset, a complete reset on your life, but not in the business domain". He worked with his trainer Randy on training and nutrition (spreadsheet to map weights, how to track, how to get in the gym, feel confident and not feel stared at), plus a dopamine detox side, monk mode side and lifestyle redesign side. Structure: first dissect your psyche to understand motivations; turn that into fuel to commit for 90 days of change; at the end, transformation of mind and body. The landing page maps day 1 through day 90: what each milestone feels and looks like, what happens to brain and body. Later he lists its protocols: dopamine detox protocol, nutrition protocol, lifestyle design protocol, training program ("the one that I personally use").
- **ejemplos:** Trainer Randy; weight-tracking spreadsheet; day 1-90 milestone landing page.
- **cita:** "this is just a 90-day reset, a complete reset on your life, but not in the business domain"
- **terminos:** Superhuman 90; dopamine detox; monk mode; lifestyle redesign
- **origen:** propia
- **nivel:** complementario
- **prerrequisitos:** U-024-106
- **fuente:** A Full Guide To Reinvent Your Entire Life (In 6-12 Months).md, 10:45-12:16, 22:29-23:12, 2025-10-19
- **tension:** ninguna



# ANEXO A — Entradas de evolución relevantes (extracto de `_libro/03b_evolucion.md`)

### EV-001 — La Matrix: de "romperla" a entenderla sin poder escapar
- **Antes:** "The Matrix Is Real (How To Break Free With Your Mind)" (2022-11-20): la Matrix es "what you think you know", la mente cerrada es aceptarla, la píldora roja saca de ella y se rompe con un "glitch" que interrumpe los ciclos robóticos (U-023-007, U-023-011, U-023-021). Ya en "How I Turned My Life Into A Video Game" (2022-12-11) toma distancia del término: "break free of the Matrix if you want to frame it that way" (U-023-229; repetido en 2024, U-003-021). En 2023 la Matrix es la rama u hoja del roble en que te condicionaron (U-017-102), y en "Life Is A Video Game (You Can't Escape The Matrix)" (2023-09-05) la percepción y el lenguaje forman un sistema cerrado (McKenna/Hoffman), una interfaz de supervivencia que, si se quitara, te volvería loco (U-023-118, U-023-119, U-023-120, U-023-121).
- **Después:** "You're Stuck In The Matrix, Here's How You Escape" (2025-10-05): "circular validation" entre todas las instituciones, tomada de actualize.org; "we live in an illusion of our own construction" (U-023-213); "we're not talking about some kind of red pill... break off from society. No": la única salida es entenderla (U-023-204), aunque más adelante dice que la agencia suele llevar al emprendimiento.
- **Cambio exacto:** de un problema de hábitos individuales que se rompe a un problema estructural y epistemológico que se comprende pero del que no se sale.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la explicita; se apoya en la condensación de actualize.org.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí. "Romper" aplica a las expectativas condicionadas (nivel psicológico) y sirve como práctica dentro del marco sistémico; "no escapar" aplica a la percepción (nivel metafísico) y a la sociedad (no hay que aislarse de ella).
- **Tipo:** refinamiento (con cambio de énfasis).


### EV-002 — Crítica a la escuela: más precisión causal y más dureza, con concesiones constantes
- **Antes:** 2022: la escuela es "a blessing and a curse", necesaria para que la sociedad funcione (U-011-054). 2023: la educación formal "has its place" (U-011-122), pero "schools can't and won't teach you" porque protegen al gobierno y la economía (U-024-087).
- **Después:** 2024: la educación general sirve "for getting people on the same page" (U-013-179); la causa raíz es que "a third party is paying" (Devon Eriksen, U-006-003, 2024-12-08); la educación actual "reflects the education of slaves" (U-006-076, 2024-07-28). 2025: el "bottom quintile" (U-010-222), la escuela que hace lo contrario de descubrir aunque da "a base operating system" (U-023-219), el "government-trained expert" (U-024-228).
- **Cambio exacto:** la crítica gana precisión causal (financiación, nivel de los más lentos, protección del poder) y dureza retórica; la concesión a las funciones básicas se mantiene.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no da razón explícita del endurecimiento; incorpora argumentos de invitados (Eriksen).
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: educación general básica (aceptada) frente a educación especializada y profesional (criticada).
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis.


### EV-003 — "Schools were created to enslave": afirmación, metáfora o propiedad del sistema
- **Antes (versión sistémica, en paralelo):** "I don't believe... someone at the top is just planning being a puppet master" (U-023-057, 2023-01-15; U-003-128, 2024-03-21). Historia de los Great Pirates: las escuelas se crearon para esclavizar a las mentes brillantes con el prestigio de la especialización, suavizado de inmediato como "just a metaphor... whether it's a conspiracy theory or not" (U-006-084, U-006-085, 2024-07-28). Mann no quería la mentalidad de esclavo, pero es "a property of the system" (U-010-220, 2025-02-23); "not a conspiracy theory" (U-021-138, 2025-04-20); "It doesn't have to be a conspiracy theory for the system to naturally take shape of the subconscious desires of the humans at the top" (U-013-209, 2025-12-21).
- **Después (versión fuerte, en paralelo):** "schools were created to keep you narrow-minded and dumb" (U-006-172, 2024-07); autocita de Purpose and Profit: "Schools were created to enslave the brightest minds... so they... didn't overthrow the true rulers", "taken quite a bit out of context" pero "largely true" (U-013-204, 2025-12-21); las escuelas públicas fueron "explicitly created to reinforce obedience" (U-023-207, 2025-10-05).
- **Cambio exacto:** no hay un cambio cronológico limpio; la tensión entre la formulación retórica fuerte y la explicación sistémica (sin titiritero) se mantiene en todo el período.
- **Razón que da el autor:** matiza que la frase fuerte es metáfora o está fuera de contexto (U-006-085, U-013-204).
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** La fuerte funciona como gancho retórico y la sistémica como explicación; el autor opta de forma declarada por la lectura metafórica.
- **Tipo:** contradicción no resuelta.


### EV-004 — Los porcentajes de "la masa" y de la distribución por etapas
- **Antes:** "90% of the time... or however many percent" (U-023-230, 2022; U-003-023, 2024); "95 percent of the population" en el bucle diario (U-011-179, 2023-04-16); "95 plus percent" y, al final del mismo video, "90 to 95%" (U-024-033, 2023-05-19); "90 to 95% of the masses" (U-024-061, 2023); "society has 95% of children" (U-012-017, 2023); "95% plus of the time" (U-012-004, 2023); "99% of people" (U-020-042, 2023; U-003-021, 2024; U-021-074, 2024); "99 of the population" que no piensa con claridad (U-026-102, 2023); "the 99% of people that do what they're told" (U-017-134, 2024-01-18). Conciencia conceptual "more so" (2023-01), "mostly" (2023-08), "95%... actually all of the time" (U-021-009, 2023-09).
- **Después:** 90/9/1 con "statistics back that a select 9%... would be better off if they got out" (U-006-158, 2024-07-13) frente a 90/9/1 presentado como opinión ("would argue"), donde el 9% "lie to themselves... or lack awareness" (U-019-037, 2024-08-11); "less than .1% of reality... closer to 0.00001%" (U-023-144, 2024); convencionales 75–80% y posconvencionales 15–20% (U-021-088, 2024-11; U-022-014, 2025-03); ~80% del contenido educativo en la etapa del logrador (U-022-017, 2025-03); 80–90% entre Human 1.0 y 2.0 (U-024-111, 2025-10); ~50% en la etapa conformista (U-013-193, 2025-12); "less than 1%" con la vida que quieres (U-025-173, 2025); "99% of people... drooling over the cat video" (U-014-116, 2026); "by 20 everyone is the same" (U-022-208, 2026).
- **Cambio exacto:** no hay un dato estable; son estimaciones retóricas que miden cosas distintas (empleo, pensamiento, condicionamiento infantil, etapas) y que no cuadran entre sí.
- **Razón que da el autor:** impresión por observación (U-012-004); en U-006-158 dice que las estadísticas lo respaldan, pero no las nombra. No las reconcilia.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** No aplica; son estimaciones del autor, no estadísticas.
- **Tipo:** inconsistencia de datos.


### EV-005 — Conformity: de enemigo a herramienta consciente
- **Antes:** "the problem is that we conform" (U-010-257, 2025-02-23); Krishnamurti: solo quien vive "in constant revolt" descubre la verdad (U-013-190, 2025-12-21). Antecedente de autocrítica: en 2023 reconoce que él mismo cae en la "selfish perspective" con el modelo de negocio de una persona (U-017-078).
- **Después:** en el mismo video (2025-12-21): "we're all conformist in some ways... it can be used like a tool", y admite que se conforma para seguir siendo relevante en YouTube (U-013-194).
- **Cambio exacto:** de condenar la conformidad a aceptarla como herramienta consciente de supervivencia.
- **Razón que da el autor:** la conformidad es una estrategia de supervivencia.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: la conformidad inconsciente que define la identidad es el problema; la elegida estratégicamente es instrumental.
- **Tipo:** refinamiento.


### EV-006 — Internet y redes sociales: cámara de socialización, mente colectiva, reflejo del usuario o agua envenenada
- **Antes:** 2022: internet es "a giant mind" que documenta la consciencia colectiva y los creadores serán "the greatest evolvers of humanity", presentado como "weird theory" (U-011-019); la respuesta al consumo masivo es individual, con "contrarian inputs" (U-007-095). Pero en "Why You Can't Be Yourself" (2022-11-13) las redes son una "socialization chamber", "socialization makes you stupid", hacen "10 times easier" el condicionamiento y son "worse all around" que la exposición física, con la autocrítica de que quizá él mismo esté condicionado (U-027-016, U-027-017, U-027-018). 2023–2024: "social media is a reflection of yourself" (U-011-198, 2023-04-16); "not an app, it's a new society", el problema es el consumo inconsciente (U-027-083, 2023-06-11); "devil or vessel" (U-021-029, 2023-09-24); "While most see social media as a net negative to humanity, I couldn't disagree more" (U-016-174, 2023-11-12); motor del 95% de su éxito (U-018-038, 2024-11-24). En paralelo: los algoritmos empeoran el condicionamiento "10 times" (U-020-060, 2023), las redes crean "children" (U-012-017, 2023) y surge la educación descentralizada (U-013-179, 2024).
- **Después:** 2025: la economía de la atención consume la capacidad cognitiva más rápido de lo que se recupera (U-015-161, 2025-09-07); internet se llena de "level one thinking" (U-015-165); "Social media and AI amplify unconsciousness", "a mimetic desire amplification machine" (U-023-209, U-023-210, 2025-10-05). 2026: los epistemic commons se envenenan (U-014-114); la forma del contenido entrena o atrofia capacidades (U-014-116); "the places that you hang out on the internet could dictate the entire outcome of your life" (U-024-153); "the religionization of everything" (U-024-145); "the modern information environment is breaking our ability to think" (U-013-237).
- **Cambio exacto:** no es lineal: del daño del medio (2022-11) a la responsabilidad del usuario y al potencial (2023–2024), y de ahí a un diagnóstico de contaminación sistémica (2025–2026), sin abandonar la idea de que crear buen contenido (ensayos) es parte de la solución.
- **Razón que da el autor:** en 2023, que el usuario decide qué alimenta al algoritmo y puede irse, bloquear o curar; en 2025, los marcos de Schmachtenberger y actualize.org. El giro de 2025–2026 no se explica.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: las redes como herramienta para quien crea y aprende frente a su efecto sobre el consumidor pasivo; el protocolo de uso consciente (menos de 30 min/día, U-027-019) hace de puente.
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis.


### EV-007 — Cómo se explica el condicionamiento (cambio de marco)
- **Antes:** 2022: redes neuronales cableadas y química corporal (Dispenza), con la aclaración "not too interested in the science... I prefer the spiritual aspect" (U-026-010); la familiaridad como supervivencia, marcada como especulación (U-026-014); metáfora del Grand Canyon (U-027-015). 2023: definición operativa, información repetida hasta volverse automática (U-023-084).
- **Después:** 2025: Krishnamurti, "thought is always conditioned and therefore mechanical" (U-023-203). 2026: memes que se autorreplican e instituciones que los protegen (U-024-143, U-024-144), el "industrial paradigm" (U-010-330, U-022-113) y la cadena información → identidad → trayectoria (U-014-115).
- **Cambio exacto:** el mecanismo pasa de lo neuronal-espiritual a lo operativo, luego a lo filosófico y por último a lo evolutivo-cultural, sin contradicción de fondo.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la explicita.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: el nivel neuronal explica el hábito individual; el memético, la persistencia cultural.
- **Tipo:** refinamiento (cambio de marco explicativo).


### EV-008 — "Curse of knowledge" con sentido propio y ambiguo
- **Antes:** "The 4-Hour Workday" (2023-01, U-017-013; reedición 2024-03, U-003-063): "the curse of knowledge" nombra el deseo natural de saber más, no su sentido habitual.
- **Después:** en la compilación "Get Ahead Of 99%..." (2024-03-21) primero (12:50) es un mecanismo negativo, aprender sin cuestionar y absorber el juego convencional; luego (46:51) es neutro o positivo: "an endless thirst to learn things that will aid in our survival" (U-003-024).
- **Cambio exacto:** no queda claro si "curse" es peyorativo; no hay corrección posterior.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no lo resuelve.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: el mismo impulso de aprender sirve para condicionarse y para construir sistemas propios.
- **Tipo:** contradicción no resuelta.


### EV-009 — Apertura mental frente a descarte tajante de los demás
- **Antes:** "everyone is right and everyone is wrong, it's all relative"; transferir la consciencia al otro y suponer una meta positiva detrás de sus actos (U-017-079, 2023-01-22); pasar "from judgment to discernment" (U-012-002, 2023-12-31); elevar la consciencia colectiva del 90–95% (U-024-061, 2023).
- **Después (y en paralelo):** "if a boomer tells you to get a real job you're on the right track" (U-011-216, 2023); ante el drama, "your only response should be: I don't care. Otherwise you're an NPC" y "you make a change by changing yourself", no proyectando creencias (U-023-182, 2024); el 99% "blabber about how you should live your life when they wasted their own... they just don't want to feel inferior to you" (U-025-173, 2025).
- **Cambio exacto:** conviven la empatía relativista y el desprecio retórico hacia "la masa".
- **Razón que da el autor:** no lo concilia explícitamente; la "deliberate ignorance" permite "entertain the thought" sin adoptarlo (U-025-174).
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: la empatía sirve para entender perspectivas; el descarte, para elegir de quién tomar consejo de vida.
- **Tipo:** contradicción no resuelta.


### EV-010 — Condena de la sociedad frente a aceptación cósmica
- **Antes / en paralelo (crítica):** la sociedad como pyramid scheme (U-023-053, 2023); las escuelas ven como amenaza "anything that gives the individual power" (U-024-087, 2023); "our botched culture" (U-027-192, 2024).
- **Después / en paralelo (aceptación):** "who's to say that Mother Nature is wrong for it being what it is today" (U-024-032, 2023); "if enough people do this then the universe will correct itself... from a universal perspective that doesn't really matter" (U-023-058, 2023); "I'm not against capitalism. I think it's going to evolve into something better" (U-010-267, 2026).
- **Cambio exacto:** no hay giro cronológico; coexisten dos registros.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no lo explica.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: el registro crítico motiva el cambio individual; el cósmico desdramatiza y quita culpa.
- **Tipo:** contradicción no resuelta.


### EV-011 — "Rigged in your favor" frente a "rigged against you"
- **Antes:** "The system is rigged, you can't change it but you can learn it, that is how you rig yourself in the system's favor" (U-023-230, 2022-12-11; repetido en 2024, U-003-023).
- **Después:** "society is rigged against you": jefe, marketing, comida, salud, círculos sociales, citas, políticos, terapeutas (U-023-212, 2025-10-05).
- **Cambio exacto:** de una actitud estratégica y lúdica (aprende las reglas) a la enumeración de cómo el sistema opera en tu contra. La propia U-023-230 registra la tensión.
- **Razón que da el autor:** ninguna explícita.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Son compatibles: el sistema está en tu contra por defecto y por eso conviene aprenderlo (primero aprender las reglas, luego crear las propias, U-010-156).
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis.


### EV-012 — La social matrix: neutral y necesaria frente a aterradora
- **Antes:** "isn't a bad thing, it creates an intellectual structure to anchor our attention" (U-018-131, 2023-11-19); la tela social es "groundless but necessary, a tool not a master" (U-017-163, 2024-01-18); "This isn't a bad thing. This is just how we learn to survive." (U-010-153, 2024-08-25).
- **Después:** el carácter circular de las fuentes de autoridad "should just terrify you" (U-023-208, 2025-10-05).
- **Cambio exacto:** de tono, no de tesis: siempre pide cuestionarla; en 2025 enfatiza el peligro.
- **Razón que da el autor:** la condensación del análisis de actualize.org.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: necesaria para sobrevivir (infancia), peligrosa si nunca se cuestiona (adultez).
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis.


### EV-015 — Apego a la identidad: no apego, identificación deliberada, identidad liviana
- **Antes:** cita a maestros espirituales: atar la identidad a algo estático causa sufrimiento innecesario (U-017-009, 2023-01; U-003-060, 2024-03); ser "like water" frente al ego colectivo (U-017-088, 2023-01); la "Christ Consciousness" como identidad "not attached to anything" (U-027-066, 2023-06). En paralelo invierte la advertencia: la identificación "can be spun for good" (U-017-009); basar la identidad en "the highest version of yourself" (U-025-024, 2023-01); Dickie Bush cita "Keep Your Identity Small" y el consistency bias (U-002-014, 2023-03), pero propone identity lists (U-002-017) y Dan les da "immediately" un giro positivo: "firm beliefs held loosely" (U-002-015).
- **Después:** Devon Eriksen advierte que cualquier "I am X" puede volverse autolimitante (U-006-029, U-006-032, U-006-033, 2024-12); "it's wise not to become dogmatic about one" y coleccionar identidades (U-018-082, 2024-05); los humanos son shape-shifters (U-025-097, 2025-03); identificarse con creencias es lo que hace dejar de pensar (U-022-100, 2026-02); incluso "weaponizing" la supervivencia deja "still playing the game… cycle of suffering" (U-024-172, 2026-05).
- **Cambio exacto:** de "no apegarse" a "apegarse conscientemente y con soltura" (2023), y luego a una advertencia creciente contra la identificación fija (2024–2026).
- **Razón que da el autor:** los humanos sobreviven en lo conceptual, así que conviene dirigir ese instinto.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: identificarse a propósito como palanca para cambiar de conducta frente a metas propias; identidad liviana frente a ideologías heredadas y para evitar el dogmatismo.
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis.


### EV-025 — Autenticidad frente a imitación
- **Antes:** el objetivo es la "consistent authenticity", que pide miles de horas (U-027-013, 2022-11).
- **Intermedio:** la intelligent imitation: imitar es inevitable y la clave es hacerlo con conciencia (U-023-092, 2023-05); responde a quienes ven un alter ego como inauténtico con su ensayo escolar (U-025-096, 2025-03).
- **Después:** polemiza con los "authenticity bros": "you imitate because you learn" (U-012-220, 2026-06; su tensión remite a U-012-122).
- **Cambio exacto:** la autenticidad deja de ser lo opuesto a imitar y pasa a ser imitar al servicio de tu "core".
- **Razón que da el autor:** se aprende imitando.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí.
- **Tipo:** refinamiento.


### EV-057 — La entropía y el caos: enemigo, recurso u oportunidad
- **Antes:** "there's an enemy we are all battling against... called entropy" (U-025-008, 2022-12; U-003-231; U-023-161, 2024-07); la mente ansía orden, seguridad y confort (U-014-008, 2022-05; U-018-076, 2024-05; U-019-070, 2024-06), lo que explica que se elija la escuela y el empleo y se consuma información que confirma la identidad (U-019-070). Pero ya en 2022-06 afirma "order stems from chaos" (U-026-034).
- **Intermedio:** "success is about entropy management": liberarla cuando la vida aburre y restringirla cuando abruma (U-019-072, 2024-06); "reject the known path, dive into the unknown... embrace the chaos" (U-021-062, 2024-11; U-025-154, 2025-12).
- **Después:** cuando la vida cae en el caos se abre la oportunidad de que emerja una versión superior (Prigogine, holones) (U-019-157, 2025-06); las estructuras ordenadas emergen de la complejidad (U-024-104, 2025-10); estar perdido es un punto de decisión y "the void is where success is born" (U-022-125, 2026-08).
- **Cambio exacto:** de la entropía como amenaza a la entropía como precondición de la transformación; el ansia de orden queda como descripción y la entrada en el caos como prescripción.
- **Razón que da el autor:** la lectura de Prigogine ("that's only half the story").
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: el orden diario protege el foco; el caos puntual permite reorganizarse a un nivel superior.
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis.


### EV-058 — "Psychic entropy": de concepto prestado a vocabulario propio
- **Antes:** "7 Books That Will Change Your Life" (2022-04): lo presenta como la lección principal de Flow (U-020-199).
- **Intermedio:** lo usa casi siempre sin atribución (U-018-007, 2022-10; U-016-057, 2024-05; U-019-070, 2024-06; U-012-121, 2025-02); junto a la cita de Csikszentmihalyi (U-018-127, 2023-11); lo glosa como término propio, "mental disorder, not a mental disorder" (U-023-166, 2024-07).
- **Después:** lo completa con "psychic negentropy" y define el sentido como "the experience of ordered consciousness" (U-014-128, 2026-04).
- **Cambio exacto:** de concepto prestado a vocabulario propio ampliado.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la da.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Mismo concepto.
- **Tipo:** refinamiento.


### EV-064 — Sistema operativo de metas → teleología
- **Antes:** "your mind is the operating system for reality... composed of goals"; con la meta equivocada todo es "100 times more difficult" (U-021-062, 2024-11).
- **Después:** mismo texto bajo el marco de la teleología y el "cosmos with a K": "if you pursue the wrong goal, you will not enjoy it" (U-025-154, 2025-12).
- **Cambio exacto:** se añade un marco filosófico explícito y se pasa de la dificultad al disfrute.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la da.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Misma tesis.
- **Tipo:** refinamiento.


### EV-103 — Videojuegos
- **Antes:** juega 2–5 horas por semana y sale cada 2–4 semanas porque tiene claras sus prioridades (U-027-165, 2023-08-30); los juegos dan riesgo y progreso falsos y su pasado de "dopamine sedation" (U-027-168, 2023-10-22).
- **Después:** si sientes la necesidad de defender los videojuegos, es señal de que no son buenos para ti, aunque hay juegos que no son "entropic" (U-024-218, 2025-09-14). Ahí atribuye a un video anterior la frase "video games are the fast food of entertainment", cuando en los videos del corpus dice "of achievement" (U-018-019).

- **Razón que da el autor:** no la da.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí.
- **Tipo:** cambio de énfasis (con inconsistencia de datos en la autocita).

## 5. Aprendizaje e IA


### EV-109 — Caminos, prescripciones y sistemas ajenos: andamio, no ley
- **Antes:** sistemas ajenos: "that's great... this is what I want you to do" y a la vez el "absolute advice" te quita la capacidad de pensar por ti mismo (U-013-031, 2022-08; U-001-096, 2024); estudiar frameworks ajenos como training wheels y luego quitarlos (U-002-105, 2023-05); "I'm kind of against prescriptions but as a beginner prescriptions are very good" (U-010-103, 2023-07-04); pedir consejo no es empezar; el consejo va al final del proceso (U-027-124, U-027-125, 2023-08-23); el big picture importa más que cualquier cadena de pasos (U-012-040, 2023-12-31).
- **Después:** "there is absolutely no set path that someone can give you... or else that's an assignment" (U-026-130, absorbido en U-026-123, 2024-03-10), mientras recomienda pagar cursos de creadores y aprender de quienes lo hacen (U-008-085, 2024-01-21; U-001-102, 2024-02-06; U-013-179, 2024-03-24) y enseña sus propios métodos; resolución parcial: el consejo da dirección y es peldaño, hay que hacerlo propio por ensayo y error (U-026-131); cursos y consejos son mapas, no territorio (U-022-024, 2025-03-16); frameworks abiertos como training wheels (U-015-112, U-015-113, 2025-02); "still don't take it as law" (U-025-174, 2025); experimentación y forma propia (U-022-183, U-024-199).
- **Cambio exacto:** convive el rechazo de cualquier camino dado con la enseñanza paso a paso.
- **Razón que da el autor:** las prescripciones valen en la etapa principiante; después, experimentación y forma propia.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Sí: el camino recibido como andamio temporal, no como destino.
- **Tipo:** contradicción no resuelta (con resolución parcial del autor).


### EV-128 — Tensiones puntuales en el aprendizaje
- **Antes / Después (varias, sin orden):** "the greatest skill" acotado a "my situation in life" (U-020-007); el video titula a la escritura como "the skill" pero la primera habilidad nombrada es "figuring it out for yourself" (U-013-005); el principio 27 (selfishness como prioridad hasta la independencia) matiza su crítica de la "selfish perspective" (U-017-150, 2024-01; frente a U-017-078); recomienda PDFs vía Z-Library y a la vez dice que "probably isn't a smart thing to do" (U-021-155); "10,000 words a day" frente a "1,000 words a day" en el mismo video, probable error (U-023-269); lista de libros de 2024 distinta de la de 2022 (U-020-146); la advertencia contra el exceso de conocimiento se matiza en seguida con el valor de la contemplación (U-011-102/U-001-063 frente a U-011-103/U-001-064).
- **Cambio exacto:** tensiones internas aisladas, registradas en las propias unidades.
- **Razón que da el autor:** no la da.
- **¿Ambas pueden aplicar a contextos distintos?:** Varía según el caso.
- **Tipo:** contradicción no resuelta / inconsistencia de datos.
